## Multiple students — Yeshiva University (December 2022) {#multiple-students-yeshiva-university-december-2022}

| Field | Value |
|---|---|
| Type | real_case |
| Institution | Yeshiva University |
| Year | 2022 |
| Category | student_disciplinary |
| Institution | Yeshiva University |

### Định nghĩa & Khái niệm

**Định nghĩa:**

> The earliest documented mass disciplinary event following ChatGPT's public release (November 30, 2022). Students submitted AI-generated responses on take-home final exams, constituting intentional misrepresentation under Yeshiva University's academic integrity policy — defined as using 'someone/something else's language' without authorization.

**Phân loại vi phạm:**

> AI-assisted dishonesty (gian lận AI): use of ChatGPT to complete take-home final exam questions. Classification as intentional misrepresentation per Yeshiva University's academic integrity policy. No contract cheating (no third party involved). No plagiarism from human sources — novel category that required policy update.

**Lịch sử khái niệm:**

> ChatGPT launched publicly November 30, 2022. The Yeshiva University incident occurred within the same month — December 2022 — making it among the first documented institutional responses to ChatGPT-facilitated cheating. The incident directly triggered revision of Yeshiva University's academic integrity policy and is frequently cited as the inaugural mass AI-cheating disciplinary case in higher education.

### Bằng chứng thực tế

**Tên trường hợp:**

> Multiple unnamed students in Professor Paula Geyh's 'Books on Books / Films on Films' course(s) at Yeshiva College, Yeshiva University, New York City. December 2022 take-home final exam. Exact number of students undisclosed publicly.

**Diễn biến:**

> In December 2022, students in Professor Paula Geyh's undergraduate English course(s) at Yeshiva College submitted take-home final exam responses. Geyh detected suspicious submissions using AI detection programs and compared student responses with ChatGPT outputs. The anomalies — uncharacteristic sophistication, atypical phrasing, and content matching AI generation patterns — led her to refer cases to Yeshiva University's undergraduate Academic Integrity Committee. Students were summoned to the committee. This became one of the first formal institutional disciplinary proceedings triggered by ChatGPT use, occurring within two weeks of ChatGPT's public launch.

**Kết quả xử lý:**

> Students were referred to the Academic Integrity Committee. Penalties under YU policy include: lowered grades, failure of assignment or course, suspension, notations on personal records, and expulsion. Specific individual outcomes were not publicly disclosed. Institutional outcome: Yeshiva University revised its undergraduate academic integrity policy to add 'generator' to the list of examples of intentional misrepresentation — explicitly covering AI text generators.

**Con số thống kê:**

> Multiple students across two course sections; exact number undisclosed. Timeline: ChatGPT launched November 30, 2022; cheating occurred December 2022; disciplinary referral December 2022 — within ~2 weeks of ChatGPT's public availability. Policy update: academic integrity policy revised January 2023.

### Tác hại & Hậu quả

**Hại cho người học:**

> Students who used ChatGPT on the final exam gained no genuine understanding of course material. The take-home exam format — designed to allow reflection and independent synthesis — was circumvented, depriving students of the learning outcome. Long-term: academic integrity notations can affect graduate school applications and careers.

**Hại cho nghiên cứu:**

> In a humanities course context, authentic literary analysis and critical thinking are the core learning objectives. AI substitution bypasses the cognitive process that constitutes the educational purpose. At scale, this degrades the quality of humanities graduates.

**Hại cho xã hội:**

> The incident generated widespread media attention and policy panic across US higher education in January 2023. It accelerated rushed AI bans at many institutions (New York City public schools banned ChatGPT days later), some of which were later reversed — demonstrating the societal disruption caused by underprepared institutional responses.

### Phát hiện & Công cụ

**Công cụ phát hiện:**

> AI detection programs (likely GPTZero or similar, exact tool unspecified in public reports) used by Professor Geyh, combined with faculty expert judgment. Take-home exam format with prior writing samples for comparison enabled detection. This is an early example of the human-AI hybrid detection approach.

**Hạn chế công cụ:**

> In December 2022, AI detection tools were in their earliest versions — GPTZero was developed by Edward Tian as a Princeton student project, also in late 2022. Turnitin had not yet released AI detection. The tools used by Geyh were rudimentary by later standards. False positive risk was not yet systematically studied; Liang et al.'s Stanford research (2023, 61.3% FP on TOEFL) came later.

**LLM Watermarking:**

> LLM watermarking did not exist in any form in December 2022 — ChatGPT had launched only days before the incident. The case pre-dates all watermarking research (Kirchenbauer et al., UMD, 2023; Aaronson/OpenAI, 2022-2023).

### Phòng ngừa & Giải pháp

**Thiết kế bài tập:**

> The Yeshiva case prompted discussion of replacing take-home exams with in-class written exams, oral defenses, or process-based assessments (e.g., requiring draft submissions, annotated bibliographies) to authenticate student work. The University of Sydney subsequently developed AI-resistant assessment frameworks.

**Chính sách trường học:**

> Pre-incident: Yeshiva University's policy covered intentional misrepresentation via 'someone/something else's language.' Post-incident: policy explicitly added 'generator' to cover AI text generators (January 2023). This was among the first documented university policy updates specifically triggered by a ChatGPT cheating incident.

**Văn hóa tổ chức:**

> The rapid detection by Professor Geyh — who proactively used detection tools within days of ChatGPT's launch — reflects individual faculty vigilance. Institutional response (policy update) demonstrates reactive adaptation. McCabe's research suggests that proactive honor code culture with education would have been more effective than post-hoc enforcement.

**AI Literacy:**

> The December 2022 timing is critical: students had essentially zero AI literacy regarding academic integrity norms for ChatGPT — the tool had been public for days. The incident highlights the need for proactive AI literacy education rather than assuming students understand new-technology boundaries.

### Khung pháp lý & Đạo đức

**Khung quốc tế:**

> ICAI's definition of academic integrity includes honesty, trust, fairness, respect, and responsibility. The Yeshiva case violates the honesty and responsibility dimensions. UNESCO's 2023 guidance on generative AI in education directly references the need for updated institutional policies — this case was among the triggers for that guidance.

**Pháp luật Việt Nam:**

> Nghị định 109/2022/NĐ-CP was issued in the same month as the Yeshiva incident (December 2022) — its provisions on scientific research integrity do not yet mention AI-generated content. The Yeshiva case illustrates the same regulatory gap present in Vietnamese law.

**Chính sách châu Á:**

> The Yeshiva University case accelerated AI policy development globally. In Asia, CUHK issued its expulsion-level AI policy in early 2023 shortly after the Yeshiva incident gained international coverage. NUS and NTU also responded in 2023.

### Góc nhìn EdTech

**AI tutor vs AI cheater:**

> The December 2022 Yeshiva case is paradigmatic AI-as-cheater use: students used ChatGPT as a substitute for their own intellectual work on a graded assessment, not as a learning scaffold. The context — humanities literary analysis — is precisely the domain where AI-generated output lacks authentic student interpretation.

**Chính sách EdTech companies:**

> OpenAI had launched ChatGPT as a research preview on November 30, 2022 with no education-specific usage policies. The Yeshiva incident occurred before any EdTech platform or university had formulated ChatGPT-specific academic integrity rules — underscoring the policy vacuum that followed ChatGPT's release.

**Đề xuất nhà nghiên cứu EdTech:**

> Key lesson from Yeshiva 2022: (1) universities must develop AI integrity policies proactively, not reactively; (2) assessment design must anticipate AI capabilities; (3) student orientation on AI ethical use should precede tool availability; (4) faculty should be equipped with detection literacy before AI tools become accessible; (5) graduated sanctions — rather than binary pass/fail — better reflect the novelty of the violation for students who had never encountered an AI policy.

### Nguồn tài liệu

**Bài báo khoa học:**

> Perkins, M., et al. (2023). Academic Integrity Considerations of AI Large Language Models in the Post-ChatGPT Era. Journal of University Teaching & Learning Practice, 20(2). Liang, W., et al. (2023). GPT detectors are biased against non-native English writers. Patterns, 4(7). DOI: 10.1016/j.patter.2023.100779.

**Sách tham khảo:**

> McCabe, D.L., Butterfield, K.D., & Treviño, L.K. (2012). Cheating in College. Johns Hopkins University Press. Gallant, T.B. (2008). Academic Integrity in the Twenty-First Century. ASHE Higher Education Report.

**Nguồn Việt Nam:**

> Nghị định 109/2022/NĐ-CP (December 2022) on scientific research integrity — concurrent with Yeshiva incident; does not address AI. VietnamNet reporting on ChatGPT in Vietnamese education (2023).

### Uncertain Fields

- con_so_thong_ke (exact number of students disciplined not publicly disclosed)
- cong_cu_phat_hien (specific AI detection tool used by Prof. Geyh not confirmed in public reports)

## Coursera AI integrity policy {#coursera-ai-integrity-policy}

| Field | Value |
|---|---|
| Type | edtech_policy |
| Category | edtech_company_policy |

### Định nghĩa & Khái niệm

**Định nghĩa:**

> Coursera's AI Academic Integrity policy is a multi-layered framework announced June 2024 that combines AI-generated post-submission viva-style questioning, plagiarism detection (Turnitin integration), and proctoring (Honorlock) to verify authentic student learning in an era of generative AI. The flagship feature is the 'AI-based Viva Exam': after a student submits a written assignment, AI analyzes the submission and generates custom follow-up questions that require the student to demonstrate genuine understanding of their own work — a digital analogue of the academic oral defense.

**Phân loại vi phạm:**

> Coursera's AI integrity framework addresses: (1) AI-assisted dishonesty — post-submission dialogue reveals whether the student can explain and defend AI-generated content; (2) Plagiarism — Turnitin integration detects textual similarity before and after submission; (3) Cheating in proctored assessments — Honorlock lockdown browser prevents unauthorized resource access; (4) Identity fraud — proctoring verifies test-taker identity. The AI Viva Exam is particularly novel: it targets AI-assisted ghostwriting by requiring real-time cognitive demonstration, not just artifact analysis.

**Lịch sử khái niệm:**

> Coursera announced its AI academic integrity suite in June 2024, following a reported surge in AI-assisted cheating on its platform since ChatGPT's release in November 2022. The AI Viva Exam concept builds on Coursera's existing 'Coach' chatbot functionality. Partnerships with Turnitin (plagiarism detection) and Honorlock (proctoring) were pre-existing and maintained alongside new AI integrity tools. The announcement came alongside other AI features including AI-Assisted Grading and Proctoring improvements.

### Bằng chứng thực tế

**Tên trường hợp:**

> Coursera AI Academic Integrity Suite, launched June 2024. Pilot institutions: select universities in India, Malaysia, and the United States. Vietnam pilot: FPT University and Van Lang University. Global platform with 148 million registered learners (2024). Partners: 325+ universities and companies offering content on Coursera.

**Diễn biến:**

> Coursera's June 2024 announcement described a GenAI-powered Academic Integrity suite comprising: (1) AI-based Viva Exam — after submission, AI analyzes free-form written assignments and grading criteria to dynamically generate custom follow-up questions testing authenticity and critical thinking behind the student's submission; students must respond to these questions, and responses are reviewed alongside the original submission; (2) AI-assisted grading with plagiarism context; (3) Enhanced Turnitin integration — students can see similarity scores before final submission and amend work; (4) Honorlock proctoring — lockdown browser for high-stakes assessments. Vietnam pilot: Coursera piloted its academic integrity tools at FPT University and Van Lang University as part of the broader Asia-Pacific rollout, with specific attention to AI-assisted academic misconduct in online degree programs.

### Tác hại & Hậu quả

**Hại cho người học:**

> Post-submission AI questioning creates a new cognitive demand on students — they must be prepared to explain their submitted work at any time. For students who engaged genuinely, this is a low-burden reinforcement of understanding. For students with test anxiety, the unexpected follow-up may be stressful. There is a potential false-positive risk: a student who wrote their own work but struggles to articulate it under pressure may be unfairly flagged. Accessibility considerations for non-native English speakers answering follow-up questions in English are important given Coursera's global learner base.

**Hại cho xã hội:**

> Positive: restores confidence in the value of online credentials by demonstrating authentic learning verification. Coursera credentials are widely used in professional hiring; if perceived as vulnerable to AI cheating, their value collapses. Negative risk: the post-submission dialogue requirement may disadvantage disadvantaged learners who have genuine knowledge but struggle with on-demand verbal/written elaboration. May also create false equivalence between Coursera's certificates and traditional university degrees if integrity systems are not rigorously validated.

### Phát hiện & Công cụ

**Công cụ phát hiện:**

> Coursera's multi-tool detection framework: (1) Turnitin AI detection — integrated into Coursera's submission pipeline; detects similarity to known sources and AI-generated content patterns; (2) AI Viva Exam — custom question generation post-submission to test authorship; (3) Honorlock — live proctoring, lockdown browser, identity verification for high-stakes exams; (4) Internal AI plagiarism detector — Coursera announced development of proprietary AI detection tool (Inside Higher Ed, June 2024). The AI Viva Exam is unique: it shifts detection from artifact analysis to cognitive demonstration.

**LLM Watermarking:**

> Coursera does not implement LLM watermarking. Watermarking (Kirchenbauer/UMD, Aaronson/OpenAI) would be complementary to Coursera's detection suite — if AI providers implement mandatory output watermarking, Coursera's Turnitin integration could detect watermarked AI-generated text with higher reliability. The AI Viva Exam is a parallel approach that authenticates the student rather than analyzing text artifacts.

### Phòng ngừa & Giải pháp

**Thiết kế bài tập:**

> The AI Viva Exam fundamentally changes Coursera's assessment model: assignments are no longer evaluated solely on the submitted artifact. The viva element creates a two-stage assessment: (1) written submission, (2) follow-up dialogue. This design mirrors process-based assessment recommendations from academic integrity researchers (Perkins et al., 2023; Eaton, 2024). For instructors, the AI-assisted grading feature provides rubric-aligned analysis, reducing grading time while maintaining quality oversight. The combination is intended to make AI-assisted dishonesty riskier and less reliable for students.

**Văn hóa tổ chức:**

> Coursera's approach is detection-and-verification-heavy rather than honor-code-cultural. The platform operates at massive scale (148M learners) where relationship-based trust is not feasible — it must rely on systemic mechanisms. The AI Viva Exam introduces a social element (dialogue) that partially addresses the anonymity problem of online assessment. Requiring students to be accountable for explaining their work in dialogue (even with an AI) is more honor-code-adjacent than purely surveillance-based.

**AI Literacy:**

> Coursera offers dedicated courses on AI literacy and ethics, including 'Generative AI for Students: Ethics & Academic Integrity' (available on the platform). The AI Viva Exam feature may inadvertently increase AI literacy: students who experience post-submission AI questioning learn that AI can analyze and probe their understanding, which may encourage more genuine engagement with assignments. Coursera's policy communications (plagiarism reminders, academic integrity training) constitute structured AI literacy interventions for learners.

### Khung pháp lý & Đạo đức

**Pháp luật Việt Nam:**

> Coursera is used extensively in Vietnam, including by FPT University and Van Lang University (explicit pilot sites for the AI integrity features). Vietnam's Nghị định 109/2022/NĐ-CP (Điều 20) establishes academic integrity requirements without specific mention of AI tools or online learning platforms. No MOET directive specifically governs the use of Coursera's AI integrity features in accredited programs. FPT and Van Lang adoption suggests institutional-level policy acceptance.

**Chính sách châu Á:**

> FPT University and Van Lang University (Vietnam): pilot sites for Coursera's AI integrity features. Indian and Malaysian universities: also pilot sites per Coursera's June 2024 announcement. NUS (Singapore): uses Coursera for some programs but has its own AI policy framework (2023/2024 disclosure framework). The Asia-Pacific focus of Coursera's pilot rollout reflects the region's large online learner population and documented concerns about AI-assisted cheating in high-volume online programs.

### Góc nhìn EdTech

**AI tutor vs AI cheater:**

> Coursera's AI Viva Exam represents a systemic attempt to shift AI from 'cheating enabler' to 'integrity verifier' — using AI to ask the questions that expose AI-assisted work. This is consistent with Rose Luckin's (UCL) argument that AI can democratize education: the AI Viva Exam makes personalized assessment (follow-up questioning) scalable in a way that human-only vivas cannot be for 148 million learners. Neil Selwyn's concern about AI undermining educational authenticity is directly addressed by the post-submission dialogue mechanism.

**Chính sách EdTech companies:**

> Coursera AI Academic Integrity suite (June 2024): (1) AI-based Viva Exam — dynamically generates custom follow-up questions from submitted work, tests authenticity and critical thinking; (2) Turnitin integration — similarity detection with pre-submission student review option; (3) Honorlock proctoring — lockdown browser and identity verification; (4) AI-assisted grading — rubric-aligned submission analysis; (5) Plagiarism acknowledgment signature. Vietnam pilot: FPT University and Van Lang University. Global pilot: select institutions in India, Malaysia, USA.

**Đề xuất nhà nghiên cứu EdTech:**

> From an EdTech researcher perspective: (1) Coursera's AI Viva Exam is an important innovation in online academic integrity — it should be rigorously evaluated in peer-reviewed research for accuracy, fairness, and student experience impact; (2) The Vietnam pilot (FPT, Van Lang) provides a valuable research opportunity for Vietnamese EdTech researchers to study AI integrity mechanisms in a national context; (3) The combination of Turnitin + Honorlock + AI Viva represents a layered defense approach consistent with best practices; (4) Risk: over-reliance on any single AI detection mechanism creates arms-race dynamics — the layered approach is more robust; (5) Institutional adoption should include student communication about the post-submission dialogue feature to maintain transparency and trust.

### Nguồn tài liệu

**Bài báo khoa học:**

> Coursera Blog (June 2024): 'Coursera launches a new suite of Academic Integrity features to help universities verify learning in an age of AI-assisted cheating.' Inside Higher Ed (June 12, 2024): 'Coursera Launches AI Plagiarism Detector.' Phil Hill & Associates (OnEdTech, 2024): 'Update on Coursera's AI and Academic Integrity Play.' Liang et al. (2023): 'GPT detectors are biased against non-native English writers' (Stanford, arXiv:2307.15395).

**Sách tham khảo:**

> Eaton, S.E. (2024). Second Handbook of Academic Integrity. Springer. Perkins, M. et al. (2023). Academic Integrity considerations of AI Large Language Models. Journal of University Teaching and Learning Practice. Bretag, T. (ed.) (2016). Handbook of Academic Integrity. Springer.

### Uncertain Fields

- ket_qua_xu_ly (pilot outcome data not yet published in peer-reviewed literature)
- con_so_thong_ke (number of institutions in AI integrity pilot not fully disclosed)
- han_che_cong_cu (no independent validation of AI Viva Exam effectiveness published)
- chinh_sach_truong_hoc (exact scope of FPT and Van Lang adoption not publicly detailed)
- khung_quoc_te (EU AI Act compliance status not publicly addressed by Coursera)
- nguon_viet_nam (no Vietnamese peer-reviewed publications on Coursera pilot as of 2025)

## Duolingo — Responsible AI Standards {#duolingo-responsible-ai-standards}

| Field | Value |
|---|---|
| Type | edtech_policy |
| Category | edtech_company_policy |

### Định nghĩa & Khái niệm

**Định nghĩa:**

> Duolingo operates two distinct AI postures that coexist within the same company: (1) AI IS the learning product — the Duolingo language learning app uses generative AI extensively for personalized instruction, conversation practice, and adaptive content generation; (2) AI is BANNED as a cheating vector in the Duolingo English Test (DET) — a high-stakes English proficiency certification used by 5,000+ universities globally for admissions. The DET's 'Responsible AI Standards' (first published April 2023, updated annually) govern how AI is used ethically in the exam context, including human-in-the-loop proctoring and an LLM cheating detection system detailed in an EMNLP 2024 paper.

**Phân loại vi phạm:**

> Duolingo's AI integrity framework specifically targets: (1) LLM-assisted cheating on open-ended writing tasks — test-takers who use AI to generate responses and copy-type them into the DET interface; (2) Cheating rings — coordinated networks of test-takers sharing answers (separate AI system, arXiv 2403.14711); (3) Identity fraud — proctoring verifies the registered test-taker is present; (4) Traditional cheating behaviors — looking away from screen, using secondary devices. The learning app context addresses AI as tutor; the DET context addresses AI as cheater.

**Lịch sử khái niệm:**

> Duolingo English Test launched in 2016 as a low-cost alternative to IELTS/TOEFL. During COVID-19 (2020–2022), DET adoption surged as test centers closed — by 2021, 5,000+ universities accepted DET. The AI integrity challenge became acute with ChatGPT's release (November 2022): test-takers could use LLMs to generate English writing that they then typed into the DET interface. Duolingo published the first DET Responsible AI Standards in April 2023. The EMNLP 2024 paper (Niu et al.) represents the first peer-reviewed publication of the LLM cheating detection methodology. Duolingo's learning app introduced AI features (DuolingoMax, GPT-4 powered Roleplay and Explain My Answer) in 2023.

### Bằng chứng thực tế

**Tên trường hợp:**

> Duolingo English Test Responsible AI Standards, first published April 2023. LLM cheating detection paper: 'Detecting LLM-Assisted Cheating on Open-Ended Writing Tasks on Language Proficiency Tests' (Niu et al., EMNLP 2024 Industry Track, ACL Anthology 2024.emnlp-industry.70). Security and Score Integrity Report published by Duolingo Research. Responsible AI for Test Equity and Quality paper (arXiv:2409.07476, 2024).

**Diễn biến:**

> DET Responsible AI Standards (2023/2024): Duolingo defined four major standards categories — (1) Validity and Reliability, (2) Fairness, (3) Privacy and Security, (4) Accountability and Transparency. Human-in-the-loop AI proctoring: AI monitors video, audio, and on-screen behavior to flag anomalies; all flagged cases are reviewed by human proctors before any certification decision. AI is used to compare test-taker writing responses against a database of internet content and historical DET writing responses, with matches flagged for human review. LLM cheating detection (EMNLP 2024): researchers developed a transformer-based classifier enhanced with contrastive learning on pairwise data and self-training on unlabeled data. The key challenge addressed: LLM-generated text that has been copy-typed by the test-taker (introducing typos and variations) is harder to detect than clean AI output. Evaluated on real-world DET dataset. The improved model outperformed baseline classifiers.

### Tác hại & Hậu quả

**Hại cho người học:**

> For DET test-takers: the multi-layer integrity system means genuine test-takers with strong English proficiency should not be penalized. However, false positive risk exists — a test-taker who writes unusually fluent English may be flagged as potentially AI-assisted. The human-in-the-loop review process is intended to catch such cases before score invalidation, but the process is not fully transparent to test-takers. For Duolingo app users: AI features are beneficial — GPT-4 powered Roleplay and Explain My Answer are pedagogically valuable without integrity risks in a non-high-stakes context.

**Hại cho nghiên cứu:**

> The EMNLP 2024 paper by Niu et al. is a significant research contribution: it is among the first peer-reviewed studies of LLM cheating detection on real-world high-stakes language test data (not synthetic). The contrastive learning + self-training approach for copy-typed AI text detection advances the state of the art. The arXiv:2409.07476 paper ('Responsible AI for Test Equity and Quality') provides a framework applicable beyond Duolingo to other high-stakes AI-assisted assessments.

**Hại cho xã hội:**

> The DET's integrity is critical for the university admissions process: 5,000+ institutions rely on DET scores to admit international students. If DET scores are perceived as vulnerable to LLM cheating, the entire certification system collapses. Duolingo's published AI standards and EMNLP research serve to publicly demonstrate rigor, maintaining institutional trust. The responsible AI standards may also influence industry norms for other testing organizations (ETS, Cambridge, British Council).

### Phát hiện & Công cụ

**Công cụ phát hiện:**

> Duolingo's DET detection stack: (1) Human-in-the-loop AI proctoring — AI monitors webcam, microphone, screen recording; anomalies flagged for human review; (2) Internet content matching — AI compares writing responses against database of online text; (3) Historical response matching — AI compares against database of prior DET submissions to detect recycled content; (4) LLM-generated text classifier (EMNLP 2024) — transformer-based model with contrastive learning, robust to copy-typed AI output; (5) Cheating ring detection (arXiv:2403.14711) — network analysis to identify coordinated cheating groups. All automated flags require human proctor review before action.

**Hạn chế công cụ:**

> The LLM detection challenge for DET is specific: test-takers copy-type AI-generated content rather than pasting it directly, introducing variations that degrade traditional AI detection accuracy. The EMNLP 2024 paper addresses this but acknowledges that as LLMs improve, the detection-evasion arms race continues. Human-in-the-loop review introduces throughput constraints — if the volume of flagged cases is high, reviewer fatigue may reduce accuracy. Fairness concern: the 'Validity and Reliability' and 'Fairness' standards in the DET RAI framework explicitly address the risk that AI proctoring may be biased against test-takers with non-standard environments, connectivity issues, or physical differences in appearance.

**LLM Watermarking:**

> Duolingo's DET approach is not based on LLM watermarking. Watermarking (Kirchenbauer/UMD, Aaronson/OpenAI) would be useful for DET if AI providers embedded detectable signals in generated text — this would simplify detection of copy-typed AI content. However, watermarking is not yet universally implemented by LLM providers. Duolingo's current system (EMNLP 2024 classifier) operates without relying on watermarks, making it applicable in the current environment.

### Phòng ngừa & Giải pháp

**Thiết kế bài tập:**

> The DET exam design itself is an integrity mechanism: the test includes speaking tasks, writing tasks, and interactive activities that must be completed in real time within a time limit, reducing (though not eliminating) the window for AI assistance. The exam includes adaptive questions that respond to test-taker performance, making preparation of fully AI-generated answers difficult. The combination of AI proctoring + LLM detection + human review is intended to make LLM-assisted cheating in the DET too risky and unreliable to attempt.

**Văn hóa tổ chức:**

> Duolingo's dual AI posture (AI-powered learning vs. AI-banned in exam) reflects a nuanced institutional stance: AI is a tool that should be used where it helps and banned where it undermines the assessment purpose. The publication of Responsible AI Standards and peer-reviewed research demonstrates a transparency and accountability culture. This contrasts with organizations that develop AI integrity tools without public disclosure of methods or limitations.

**AI Literacy:**

> Duolingo's app is explicitly designed to build AI literacy through use — students interact with AI conversation partners and receive AI-powered explanations, normalizing appropriate AI-assisted learning. The DET Responsible AI Standards publication educates the testing community about AI in high-stakes assessment contexts. Duolingo publishes detailed research reports (arXiv, EMNLP) that contribute to public understanding of LLM cheating detection — an important form of institutional AI literacy contribution.

### Khung pháp lý & Đạo đức

**Pháp luật Việt Nam:**

> DET is accepted by Vietnamese universities and is used by Vietnamese students applying to international programs. Vietnam's Nghị định 109/2022/NĐ-CP focuses on scientific research integrity, not language proficiency testing. No MOET regulation specifically governs DET or similar online testing in Vietnam. The integrity mechanisms described in the DET Responsible AI Standards are relevant to Vietnam's broader discussion of AI use in standardized testing.

**Chính sách châu Á:**

> DET is widely used by Asian students for university admissions. NUS (Singapore), NTU (Singapore), and major Chinese, South Korean, and Japanese universities accept DET scores. The LLM cheating challenge is particularly acute in Asian markets where AI-assisted test preparation services are commercially available. Duolingo's EMNLP 2024 paper dataset includes real-world DET submissions from global test-takers including Asian candidates — providing one of the few large-scale datasets of LLM cheating in high-stakes Asian-relevant testing contexts.

### Góc nhìn EdTech

**AI tutor vs AI cheater:**

> Duolingo is the clearest example of the AI-tutor-vs-AI-cheater tension within a single company's product portfolio. The Duolingo app embodies Rose Luckin's (UCL) democratizing AI tutor — making language learning accessible globally. The DET embodies Neil Selwyn's concern — AI enabling fraudulent credentials. Duolingo's response is not to choose one framing but to design each product context appropriately: AI-powered where it serves learning, AI-excluded where it undermines assessment validity. The Responsible AI Standards represent Duolingo's public articulation of this context-dependent policy.

**Chính sách EdTech companies:**

> Duolingo two-context AI framework: (1) Duolingo App — AI IS the product: GPT-4 powered Roleplay (DuolingoMax), Explain My Answer, adaptive learning engine; AI used extensively to personalize instruction; no integrity concerns in informal learning context; (2) Duolingo English Test (DET) — AI is BANNED as a cheating vector: human-in-the-loop proctoring, LLM cheating detection (EMNLP 2024), internet content matching, historical response matching, cheating ring detection; all AI flags require human review; (3) Responsible AI Standards: four categories published April 2023, updated annually — Validity & Reliability, Fairness, Privacy & Security, Accountability & Transparency.

**Đề xuất nhà nghiên cứu EdTech:**

> From an EdTech researcher perspective: (1) Duolingo's dual AI posture should be a model for the industry: AI literacy tools (app) and high-stakes assessment (DET) require fundamentally different integrity frameworks; (2) The EMNLP 2024 paper by Niu et al. represents best practice for responsible disclosure of AI integrity system methodology — other EdTech companies should publish similar research; (3) The human-in-the-loop requirement for all AI flags is the correct approach for high-stakes decisions — automated-only decisions in high-stakes assessment are ethically problematic; (4) The contrastive learning approach for detecting copy-typed AI text should be evaluated for application in university plagiarism detection; (5) DET's Responsible AI Standards should be adopted as a template for other certification bodies (Cambridge, IELTS, TOEFL) facing similar LLM cheating challenges.

### Nguồn tài liệu

**Bài báo khoa học:**

> Niu, C., Yancey, K.P., Liu, R., Baig, M.B., Horie, A.K., & Sharpnack, J. (2024). Detecting LLM-Assisted Cheating on Open-Ended Writing Tasks on Language Proficiency Tests. EMNLP 2024 Industry Track. ACL Anthology: 2024.emnlp-industry.70. Duolingo (2024). Responsible AI for Test Equity and Quality: The Duolingo English Test as a Case Study. arXiv:2409.07476. Duolingo (2024). Duolingo English Test: Security and Score Integrity Research Report. Human-in-the-Loop AI for Cheating Ring Detection. arXiv:2403.14711 (2024).

**Sách tham khảo:**

> Eaton, S.E. (2024). Second Handbook of Academic Integrity. Springer. Gallant, T.B. (2008). Academic Integrity in the Twenty-First Century. Jossey-Bass. Duolingo English Test Responsible AI Standards (April 2023, updated 2024). URL: duolingo-papers.s3.us-east-1.amazonaws.com/other/Duolingo+English+Test+Responsible+AI.pdf

### Uncertain Fields

- ket_qua_xu_ly (exact number of invalidated DET scores not publicly disclosed)
- con_so_thong_ke (DET annual test volume and EMNLP 2024 model AUC/F1 on exam conditions not cited in public summaries)
- chinh_sach_truong_hoc (no university publicly withdrew DET acceptance due to LLM concerns — verified as of 2025)
- khung_quoc_te (EU AI Act compliance details not publicly addressed by Duolingo)

## Khan Academy — Khanmigo {#khan-academy-khanmigo}

| Field | Value |
|---|---|
| Type | edtech_policy |
| Category | edtech_company_policy |

### Định nghĩa & Khái niệm

**Định nghĩa:**

> Khanmigo is Khan Academy's AI-powered tutoring and teaching assistant, launched March 2023, built on OpenAI's GPT-4. It is designed around a Socratic pedagogy: the AI guides students through reasoning by asking questions rather than providing direct answers. The key academic integrity design principle is that Khanmigo cannot be used as a cheating tool — it refuses to write essays, complete assignments, or produce work that students would submit as their own. This makes Khanmigo an example of 'integrity-by-design' AI in education.

**Phân loại vi phạm:**

> Khanmigo's policy framework addresses: (1) AI-assisted dishonesty — by design refusal to generate submittable academic work; (2) Plagiarism prevention — flags pasted external text (15+ words) for teacher review in Writing Coach; (3) Transparency — full conversation logs accessible to teachers, parents, and administrators, eliminating the hidden AI use that characterizes most AI-assisted academic dishonesty.

**Lịch sử khái niệm:**

> Khanmigo was announced at TED in March 2023 by Sal Khan. It launched in beta March 2023, initially at $4/month for individual users, later made free for students in the US (2024). The product was built in partnership with OpenAI following OpenAI's nonprofit donation to Khan Academy. The Socratic design philosophy predates AI — Khan Academy's pedagogical approach has always emphasized guided discovery over direct instruction. Khanmigo applies this to generative AI, framing it as proof that AI can be deployed responsibly in education.

### Bằng chứng thực tế

**Tên trường hợp:**

> Khan Academy — Khanmigo, launched March 2023. Global deployment to K-12 students and teachers across Khan Academy's platform. Available as both student-facing tutor and teacher-facing assistant. Free to US students; international availability expanded through Microsoft partnership (2024).

**Diễn biến:**

> Khan Academy partnered with OpenAI to build Khanmigo, with explicit design constraints to prevent academic dishonesty. Core mechanism: Khanmigo uses a Socratic persona — instead of answering questions directly, it responds with guiding questions that prompt the student to think through problems themselves. For writing tasks, Khanmigo's Writing Coach helps students understand prompts, create outlines, and get feedback on drafts — but will not write the essay itself. If a student pastes 15 or more words from an external source, the passage is flagged for teacher review. All AI-student conversations are logged and visible to educators and parents. This transparency feature fundamentally changes the accountability structure: students cannot use Khanmigo covertly, unlike ChatGPT or other general-purpose AI tools.

**Kết quả xử lý:**

> No formal disciplinary case — Khanmigo's design prevents misconduct rather than detecting it afterward. Teacher access to full conversation logs means any attempt to misuse Khanmigo (e.g., asking it to write an essay) is visible to educators. The system will notify teachers if safety issues arise with minor users. Khan Academy reports positive reception from educators, with Khanmigo earning 'rave reviews from teachers' since its March 2023 launch. The product is cited frequently in EdTech literature as a model of responsible AI design for education.

### Tác hại & Hậu quả

**Hại cho người học:**

> Khanmigo's Socratic design is explicitly intended to benefit learners by developing genuine understanding rather than enabling shortcut-taking. The harm risk is minimal by design: students who want to cheat via Khanmigo will be unable to, and those who engage honestly receive pedagogically effective guidance. Potential limitation: students accustomed to immediate AI-generated answers may find Khanmigo's question-based responses frustrating, potentially leading them to use other AI tools instead.

**Hại cho xã hội:**

> Positive societal impact: Khanmigo demonstrates that AI tools can be deployed in education without enabling mass academic dishonesty, countering the narrative that AI inevitably undermines educational standards. It also addresses equity concerns by providing free AI tutoring to US students who cannot afford private tutors. Risk: if Khanmigo's Socratic approach is perceived as less useful than ChatGPT, students may migrate to less controlled platforms.

### Phát hiện & Công cụ

**Công cụ phát hiện:**

> Khanmigo is a prevention tool, not a detection tool. Its built-in detection mechanisms are: (1) text-paste flagging — 15+ words pasted from external sources are flagged for teacher review in Writing Coach; (2) conversation logging — all AI-student interactions are recorded and visible to teachers, parents, and administrators; (3) safety alerts — the system notifies teachers of potential safety issues with minor users. Unlike Turnitin or GPTZero, Khanmigo's detection is embedded in the workflow rather than applied after submission.

**Hạn chế công cụ:**

> Khanmigo's Socratic refusal design has a key limitation: students can use Khanmigo legitimately for tutoring while simultaneously using ChatGPT or another tool to write their actual submission. Khanmigo only controls what happens within its own platform — it cannot detect or prevent use of external AI tools. This means Khanmigo's integrity guarantee applies only to work produced through the Khanmigo interface, not to student work generally. The system is also only as effective as educator engagement with the conversation logs.

**LLM Watermarking:**

> Khanmigo does not implement LLM watermarking. Its integrity mechanism is conversation transparency and Socratic refusal rather than artifact-based detection. Watermarking (Kirchenbauer/UMD, Aaronson/OpenAI) is designed for detecting AI-generated text in arbitrary submissions — a different point in the integrity workflow. The two approaches are complementary: Khanmigo prevents misconduct within its platform; watermarking could detect AI-generated text submitted from other tools.

### Phòng ngừa & Giải pháp

**Thiết kế bài tập:**

> Khanmigo Writing Coach supports process-based writing pedagogy: students work through prompts, outlines, and draft feedback stages with AI guidance, generating a traceable writing process. Teachers receive logs of the entire process, not just the final product. This aligns with the broader recommendation in academic integrity literature (Eaton, 2024; Perkins et al., 2023) to assess process rather than only product. Khanmigo's design is effectively a process-based submission tool with built-in Socratic scaffolding.

**Chính sách trường học:**

> Khanmigo is used in K-12 school settings across the US and internationally. Schools that adopt Khanmigo receive a teacher dashboard with student activity monitoring. Khan Academy's terms of service and educator guidelines specify appropriate use. No single institutional policy document comparable to a university academic integrity policy governs Khanmigo — it operates within existing school AI policies. The product is frequently cited by educators as an example of 'approved AI use' that can be incorporated into school AI policies.

**Văn hóa tổ chức:**

> Khanmigo operationalizes McCabe's honor code culture principles at the product design level: rather than relying on student self-regulation or enforcement mechanisms, the system embeds integrity into the interaction design itself. The transparency of conversation logs creates mutual accountability without adversarial surveillance — both students and teachers can see what was discussed, normalizing open AI-assisted learning rather than hiding it.

**AI Literacy:**

> Khanmigo is an AI literacy tool by design: students learn how to engage with AI through Socratic dialogue, developing critical thinking about their own reasoning rather than receiving pre-packaged answers. This models appropriate AI use — AI as a thinking partner rather than an answer machine. UNESCO Art. 4 and EU AI Act emphasis on AI literacy align with Khanmigo's pedagogical approach. Sal Khan has explicitly stated the goal is to model how AI should be used in education, not just to deliver content.

### Khung pháp lý & Đạo đức

**Pháp luật Việt Nam:**

> Khanmigo is available in Vietnam via Khan Academy's international platform and the Microsoft partnership. Vietnamese regulatory framework (Nghị định 109/2022, Điều 20) does not specifically address AI tutoring tools. No Ministry of Education (MOET) guidance specifically endorses or restricts Khanmigo use in Vietnamese schools. The platform's Socratic, non-essay-writing design would likely be viewed favorably under Vietnam's academic integrity norms.

### Góc nhìn EdTech

**AI tutor vs AI cheater:**

> Khanmigo is the paradigmatic example of Rose Luckin's (UCL) positive framing — AI as democratizing tutor — operationalized in a product that is structurally resistant to the cheating use case Selwyn (Monash) warns about. Sal Khan explicitly argues that AI of the Khanmigo type will 'democratize' access to personalized tutoring that only wealthy families previously could afford. The product demonstrates that the AI tutor / AI cheater binary is not inevitable — design choices determine which the tool becomes.

**Chính sách EdTech companies:**

> Khanmigo's policy framework: (1) Socratic design — AI cannot complete assignments for students; (2) Essay refusal — Khanmigo will not write essays or produce submittable academic work; (3) Text-paste flagging — 15+ words from external sources flagged for teacher review; (4) Full conversation transparency — all AI-student dialogue visible to educators, parents, administrators; (5) Safety alerts for minor users. Partnership with OpenAI (technical infrastructure) and Microsoft (distribution). Free for US students since 2024. Available to teachers as classroom assistant with different feature set.

**Đề xuất nhà nghiên cứu EdTech:**

> From an EdTech researcher perspective: (1) Khanmigo demonstrates that integrity-by-design is technically feasible for AI tutoring products — this should become a design standard, not a competitive differentiator; (2) EdTech platforms that allow AI to produce submittable work should face institutional scrutiny; (3) Schools should prefer AI tools with conversation transparency and Socratic constraints over unconstrained general-purpose AI; (4) Research needed: longitudinal studies on whether Khanmigo users show different learning outcomes and academic integrity behavior compared to users of unconstrained AI tools; (5) The AIAS framework (Perkins) should include an assessment of whether EdTech AI tools implement integrity-by-design principles.

### Nguồn tài liệu

**Bài báo khoa học:**

> Perkins, M. et al. (2023). Academic Integrity considerations of AI Large Language Models in the post-ChatGPT era. Journal of University Teaching and Learning Practice. Skywork AI (2024): 'Khanmigo Deep Dive: How Khan Academy's AI is Shaping the Future of Education.' Khan Academy Help Center: 'What is Khan Academy's approach to responsible AI development?' (support.khanacademy.org). Sal Khan, TED 2023 talk: 'How AI could save (not destroy) education.'

**Sách tham khảo:**

> Eaton, S.E. (2024). Second Handbook of Academic Integrity. Springer. McCabe, D.L. (2012). Cheating in College. Johns Hopkins UP. Khan, S. (2012). The One World Schoolhouse. Twelve/Hachette.

## Bretag's 5 Core Elements of Exemplary AI Policy {#bretags-5-core-elements-of-exemplary-ai-policy}

| Field | Value |
|---|---|
| Type | framework |
| Year | 2011 |
| Author | Tracey Bretag |

### Định nghĩa & Khái niệm

**Định nghĩa:**

> Bretag et al. (2011) identified five core elements that characterize exemplary academic integrity policies, derived from a systematic analysis of all 40 Australian university academic integrity policies. The framework — often called the AARDS framework or '5 Core Elements' — specifies what makes a policy effective at promoting integrity rather than merely punishing violations. The five elements are: Access (policy is accessible and communicated to all stakeholders), Approach (proactive, educative stance rather than purely punitive), Responsibility (shared — students, faculty, and institutions all bear responsibility), Detail (clear, specific definitions and procedures), and Support (resources and support structures accompany the policy). Though originally developed for academic integrity broadly, the framework is directly applicable to AI-era policy design.

**Phân loại vi phạm:**

> Bretag's framework is an evaluative/prescriptive meta-framework for policy quality rather than a violation taxonomy. Policy failures can be diagnosed through missing elements: (1) Access failure — policy exists but students/faculty are unaware of it; (2) Approach failure — policy is purely punitive with no educational component; (3) Responsibility failure — only students are held accountable, not the institution or faculty who designed AI-vulnerable assessments; (4) Detail failure — policy does not define what AI use is prohibited or permitted in specific contexts; (5) Support failure — no AI literacy training, no advising resources, no appeals process. AI-era policies that prohibit AI but provide no AI literacy support fail the Approach and Support elements.

**Lịch sử khái niệm:**

> The 5 Core Elements framework was published in the International Journal for Educational Integrity in 2011 (IJEI 7(2):3-12). Bretag led a research project auditing all 40 Australian university academic integrity policies — producing the most comprehensive empirical foundation for what effective policies contain. The framework predates widespread AI awareness but has proven remarkably prescient: every element it identifies is violated by the wave of reactive AI prohibition policies issued in 2023-2024. Bretag extended this work in the 'Handbook of Academic Integrity' (Springer, 2016), the authoritative academic integrity reference.

### Bằng chứng thực tế

**Tên trường hợp:**

> The 5 Core Elements framework has been applied to policy audit in Australia, UK, Canada, and internationally. Direct applications: Australian government higher education reviews cited Bretag's elements; UK QAA academic integrity guidance (2023) aligns with the framework; multiple Canadian university policy reviews reference Bretag et al. (2011). The framework was also applied to analyze institutional AI policies in 2023-2024, finding that most reactive AI prohibition policies failed on the Approach and Support elements.

**Diễn biến:**

> Bretag's methodology: collected all 40 Australian university academic integrity policies, developed a coding framework based on policy science literature, identified patterns across policies, and inductively derived the 5 elements that distinguished exemplary from inadequate policies. Key finding: most policies at the time were detailed on violations and punishments (Detail — procedures) but weak on educational approach, shared responsibility, and student support. The framework was subsequently validated across multiple national contexts.

**Kết quả xử lý:**

> The 5 Core Elements framework became the standard for academic integrity policy audit internationally. Bretag's Handbook of Academic Integrity (2016) is the most-cited reference work in the field. The framework has been used by higher education regulatory bodies in Australia, UK, and Canada as a quality benchmark. In the AI era, researchers applying Bretag's framework to university AI policies (2023-2024) consistently found deficiencies in the Approach element (educational support) and the Responsibility element (institutional vs. student accountability).

**Con số thống kê:**

> Based on analysis of 40 Australian university policies; published 2011 IJEI 7(2):3-12; 5 elements; framework has been applied in policy audits across Australia, UK, Canada [uncertain: exact count of institutional applications]; Bretag's Handbook (2016) is one of the most cited works in academic integrity research with 500+ citations [uncertain: exact citation count].

### Tác hại & Hậu quả

**Hại cho người học:**

> Bretag's Responsibility element specifically identifies shared responsibility: students are harmed when institutions hold them solely accountable for integrity violations while the institution designs AI-vulnerable assessments and provides no AI literacy training. The Support element ensures students have resources to act with integrity — when support is absent, students face integrity challenges without institutional scaffolding.

**Hại cho nghiên cứu:**

> The Detail element requires policies to clearly define what counts as a violation in research contexts — including AI-assisted writing, hallucinated citations, and undisclosed AI use. Without detail, researchers face ambiguity about permissible AI assistance in manuscript preparation, data analysis, and literature review — leading to inconsistent enforcement that undermines research integrity norms.

**Hại cho xã hội:**

> Weak policies (failing Bretag's elements) produce credential inflation without genuine competency assurance. The Access element ensures all students know the rules — without access, enforcement is inequitable (penalizing students unaware of policies). The Approach element protects educational value: punitive-only approaches push violations underground rather than building genuine integrity.

### Phát hiện & Công cụ

**Công cụ phát hiện:**

> Bretag's framework does not address detection tools directly (2011 predates AI detectors). However, the Detail element requires policies to specify how violations are detected and adjudicated. The Approach element cautions against over-reliance on surveillance and detection at the expense of education — directly relevant to the Turnitin/GPTZero debate. An exemplary policy specifies detection tools, their limitations, and the evidentiary standards for using detector output in disciplinary proceedings.

**Hạn chế công cụ:**

> The Approach and Support elements are most relevant to detection tool limitations. An exemplary AI integrity policy (meeting Bretag's standards) would: (1) Specify detection tools as one input, not definitive evidence; (2) Include ESL/EAL student support to reduce false-positive risk; (3) Provide faculty training on detection tool limitations (Liang et al., 2023); (4) Establish a support pathway for students wrongly accused by AI detectors.

**LLM Watermarking:**

> LLM watermarking would satisfy Bretag's Detail element if policies specify it as an integrity mechanism. However, the Approach element requires educational rather than purely surveillance-based solutions — watermarking is a detection technology, not an educational intervention. An exemplary policy combining watermarking with AI literacy education would better satisfy all 5 elements.

### Phòng ngừa & Giải pháp

**Thiết kế bài tập:**

> Bretag's framework applies to assessment design through the Responsibility element: institutions that design AI-vulnerable assessments (generic essay prompts, low-stakes take-home work) share responsibility for the violations they invite. Exemplary policy (meeting the Detail element) specifies assessment-level expectations — what AI use is permissible in each task type. This directly motivates AIAS-like tiered assessment design.

**Chính sách trường học:**

> The 5 Core Elements are the de facto standard for academic integrity policy quality. Applied to major AI policies: Stanford's contextual AI use policy meets Access and Detail but is weak on institutional Responsibility (it focuses on student obligations). NUS's disclosure framework meets Approach (educative) and Support (training provided) but Detail varies by faculty. Bretag's framework reveals that no university has yet produced a fully exemplary AI integrity policy meeting all 5 elements consistently.

**Văn hóa tổ chức:**

> The Approach element is Bretag's most direct contribution to organizational culture theory: effective policies are proactive and educational, not purely punitive. This aligns with McCabe's finding that honor code cultures outperform enforcement-only cultures. Bretag provides the policy-level mechanism: institutional policies must embody an educative approach to produce integrity culture, not just integrity compliance.

**AI Literacy:**

> The Support element directly requires AI literacy resources as part of any exemplary AI integrity policy. Students must have access to training on what AI tools do, when use is appropriate, and how to attribute AI contributions. An AI integrity policy that prohibits AI without providing AI literacy support fails the Support element. This aligns with UNESCO 2023 guidance and EU AI Act Article 4.

### Khung pháp lý & Đạo đức

**Khung quốc tế:**

> Bretag's 5 Core Elements are the policy quality standard that UNESCO 2023, EU AI Act, and ICAI guidance implicitly require. UNESCO 2023's recommendation to support students in using AI 'effectively, ethically and transparently' maps to Approach + Support. EU AI Act Article 4 (AI literacy) maps to Support. ICAI's 6 values provide the normative content; Bretag's elements provide the policy architecture. The frameworks are complementary at different levels of analysis.

**Pháp luật Việt Nam:**

> Vietnam's Decree 109/2022 Dieu 20 fails at least 3 of Bretag's 5 elements when applied to AI integrity: (1) Access — no specific AI guidance communicated to students; (2) Approach — purely punitive framing; (3) Support — no AI literacy training mandated. The Bretag framework provides a diagnostic tool for MOET to assess and improve Vietnam's academic integrity regulatory framework. A Bretag-compliant Vietnamese AI integrity policy would require cross-ministry coordination (MOET + MOST) for the Support element.

**Chính sách châu Á:**

> Asian institutional AI policies analyzed through Bretag's elements: CUHK 2023 prohibition policy — Detail (clear prohibition) but weak on Approach (punitive) and Support (no AI literacy mandate); NUS disclosure framework — strong on Approach and Support, adequate on Detail; NTU zero-marks policy — Detail (specific consequence) but weak on Support. The Bretag framework explains why NUS's approach is more sustainable than CUHK's or NTU's: it satisfies more of the 5 elements.

### Góc nhìn EdTech

**AI tutor vs AI cheater:**

> Bretag's framework reframes the tutor/cheater debate as a policy design question: an institution with an exemplary policy (meeting all 5 elements) creates conditions where AI can legitimately function as a tutor (because students understand appropriate use through Access and Support) and cannot easily function as a cheater (because policies Detail specific prohibitions with educational Approach). The dichotomy dissolves under good policy design.

**Chính sách EdTech companies:**

> Bretag's Support element requires that EdTech platforms used in academic contexts provide integrity-promoting features. Khan Academy Khanmigo's Socratic design supports the Approach element (educative rather than enabling). Turnitin's integration with educational feedback tools attempts to meet Approach. However, most AI writing tools (ChatGPT, Gemini, Copilot) provide no academic integrity support features — violating what Bretag's Support element would require of institutional policies governing their use.

**Đề xuất nhà nghiên cứu EdTech:**

> Applying Bretag's framework to EdTech AI integrity research: (1) Use the 5 Core Elements as an audit instrument to evaluate university AI policies systematically; (2) Recommend that Vietnamese universities audit their Decree 109 compliance against Bretag's elements; (3) Prioritize the Support element in Vietnamese policy development — AI literacy is the most actionable reform; (4) The Responsibility element provides the theoretical basis for arguing that universities (not only students) bear accountability for AI-enabled academic fraud when assessment design is AI-vulnerable; (5) Publish a Bretag-framework audit of the top 20 Vietnamese universities' AI integrity policies as a contribution to the international literature.

### Nguồn tài liệu

**Bài báo khoa học:**

> Bretag, T., Mahmud, S., Wallace, M., Walker, R., James, C., Green, M., ... & Patey, R. (2011). Core elements of exemplary academic integrity policy in Australian higher education. International Journal for Educational Integrity, 7(2), 3-12. Bretag, T., et al. (2014). Defining academic integrity: A taxonomy of academic misconduct. Studies in Higher Education. Newton, P.M. & Xiromeriti, M. (2023). GenAI and the post-plagiarism university. Frontiers in Education.

**Sách tham khảo:**

> Bretag, T. (ed.) (2016). Handbook of Academic Integrity. Springer. McCabe, D.L. et al. (2012). Cheating in College. Johns Hopkins UP. Eaton, S.E. (ed.) (2024). Second Handbook of Academic Integrity. Springer. Gallant, T.B. (2008). Academic Integrity in the Twenty-First Century. ASHE Higher Education Report.

### Other Info

**Source:** Bretag et al. (2011) IJEI 7(2):3-12 — analysis of all 40 Australian university policies

## Postplagiarism Framework (Eaton, 2023) {#postplagiarism-framework-eaton-2023}

| Field | Value |
|---|---|
| Type | framework |
| Year | 2023 |
| Author | Sarah Elaine Eaton |
| Doi | https://link.springer.com/article/10.1007/s40979-023-00144-1 |

### Định nghĩa & Khái niệm

**Định nghĩa:**

> Eaton's Postplagiarism framework proposes that traditional concepts of plagiarism are becoming obsolete in the age of generative AI. The term 'postplagiarism' does not mean the end of ethical standards; rather, it signals a paradigmatic shift in how authorship, originality, and academic integrity must be conceptualized. In a postplagiarism world, Human-AI hybrid writing is expected and normative, but the writer retains full ethical responsibility for the final output regardless of the degree of AI assistance.

**Phân loại vi phạm:**

> Under the postplagiarism framework, violations are reframed around responsibility and attribution rather than mechanical text-matching. Key categories: (1) Responsibility displacement — submitting AI output while disavowing authorship; (2) Attribution failure — not disclosing AI tools used; (3) Competence misrepresentation — claiming skills one does not possess because AI performed the task; (4) Language barrier exploitation — using AI to mask deficits that assessments are intended to measure. Traditional plagiarism (copying human text without attribution) remains a violation but is treated as a subset of a broader responsibility framework.

**Lịch sử khái niệm:**

> The term 'postplagiarism' was coined and theorized by Eaton in this 2023 article published in the International Journal for Educational Integrity. The concept builds on Eaton's earlier work including 'Plagiarism in Higher Education: Tackling Tough Topics in Academic Integrity' (2021). It directly responds to the disruption caused by ChatGPT's public release in November 2022 and the inadequacy of existing plagiarism frameworks for AI-generated content.

### Bằng chứng thực tế

**Tên trường hợp:**

> The postplagiarism framework is primarily theoretical but directly informed by institutional responses in 2023. Key contexts: University of Sydney AI integrity policy (2023), Vanderbilt University ChatGPT statement (2023), and the broader wave of university policy revisions following ChatGPT release in late 2022.

**Diễn biến:**

> Eaton argues that GenAI tools like ChatGPT fundamentally change the authorship landscape: (1) AI can produce human-indistinguishable text, making traditional text-matching plagiarism detection inadequate; (2) AI can assist non-native speakers in producing fluent academic prose, raising questions about what assessments actually measure; (3) Creativity is not diminished but augmented by AI — just as word processors changed writing, AI changes composition. The six tenets provide a new normative foundation that retains ethical accountability while abandoning outdated definitions.

**Kết quả xử lý:**

> The postplagiarism framework has been widely cited in the academic integrity literature since 2023. It influenced the development of attribution standards in institutional AI policies. Eaton extended this work in the 'Second Handbook of Academic Integrity' (2024, Springer), which includes a chapter on AI and postplagiarism. Multiple universities referenced the framework when revising their honor codes in 2023-2024.

**Con số thống kê:**

> Published 2023 in IJEI; 6 core tenets; DOI: 10.1007/s40979-023-00144-1; Eaton's broader academic integrity work spans 2+ books (2021, 2024) and numerous journal articles. The article received significant citation attention given the timing (post-ChatGPT) [uncertain: exact citation count].

### Tác hại & Hậu quả

**Hại cho người học:**

> The postplagiarism framework identifies a core harm: students who outsource cognitive work to AI fail to develop genuine competencies. Tenet 4 ('Can outsource writing to AI but not responsibility') implies that students who do so face a competence gap — they hold credentials without corresponding skills, which will manifest in professional contexts. Tenet 2 addresses AI-enhanced creativity positively, suggesting that AI used as a cognitive scaffold (rather than a replacement) can benefit learners.

**Hại cho nghiên cứu:**

> Postplagiarism raises research integrity concerns around attribution and hallucinated citations. If researchers use AI to draft papers without disclosure (violating Tenet 6 on attribution), the scholarly record becomes opaque. Eaton's framework implies that all AI-assisted academic work requires provenance documentation — the same standards that apply to data and methods must apply to text generation.

**Hại cho xã hội:**

> The postplagiarism era risks devaluing academic credentials if degrees are attained through AI-outsourced work without genuine competency development. Eaton argues this requires reimagining assessment — not simply banning AI — to ensure credentials reflect genuine human capability. The language-barrier dimension (Tenet 3) has social equity implications: AI assistance for ESL students raises both fairness and authenticity concerns.

### Phát hiện & Công cụ

**Công cụ phát hiện:**

> The postplagiarism framework explicitly challenges the premise of AI detection tools. If Human-AI hybrid writing becomes the norm (Tenet 1), then binary detection (AI vs. human) becomes both technically and normatively inadequate. The framework implies that attribution-based approaches — declaration systems, portfolio evidence, process documentation — are more defensible than detection tools.

**Hạn chế công cụ:**

> Eaton's framework aligns with Liang et al.'s (Stanford, 2023) findings that AI detectors produce high false positive rates for ESL writers (61.3%). Tenet 3 (AI can overcome language barriers) and Tenet 5 (attribution remains important) together suggest that the appropriate response to language-barrier cases is disclosure and attribution policy, not detection and punishment.

**LLM Watermarking:**

> LLM watermarking (Kirchenbauer/UMD, Aaronson/OpenAI) would be technically consistent with the postplagiarism framework's attribution tenet (Tenet 5) — watermarks enable provenance tracking. However, Eaton's framework is more concerned with ethical responsibility than technical enforcement; watermarking addresses detection but not the deeper issue of responsibility displacement.

### Phòng ngừa & Giải pháp

**Thiết kế bài tập:**

> The postplagiarism framework most directly supports process-based and reflective assessment designs: (1) Staged submissions with process documentation; (2) Reflective journals documenting human cognitive contribution; (3) Oral defense of AI-assisted work; (4) Portfolio-based assessment demonstrating skill development over time. These designs shift evaluation from text output to demonstrable competence and reflective engagement — directly responding to Tenet 4 on responsibility.

**Chính sách trường học:**

> The postplagiarism framework suggests a tiered disclosure policy rather than blanket prohibition. Eaton's approach aligns with Perkins' AIAS framework (Tiers 3-5 allowing AI with disclosure or integration). Stanford's contextual AI use policy, NUS's disclosure framework (2023-2024), and University of Sydney's AI integrity policy all reflect postplagiarism thinking — retaining ethical standards while adapting to AI reality.

**Văn hóa tổ chức:**

> Eaton's framework requires a cultural shift: institutions must move from detection-punishment models to responsibility-attribution models. This aligns with McCabe's honor culture research — students who understand the why of integrity (including AI responsibility) are more likely to comply than those who face only enforcement. Postplagiarism requires faculty education as much as student education.

**AI Literacy:**

> Tenet 6 (rethinking historical definitions of plagiarism) directly requires AI literacy education. Students and faculty must understand what AI tools do, how to attribute AI contributions, and what 'authorship' means in a hybrid writing environment. This maps to UNESCO 2023 guidance and EU AI Act Article 4 on mandatory AI literacy.

### Khung pháp lý & Đạo đức

**Khung quốc tế:**

> Eaton's postplagiarism framework is a scholarly contribution from within the academic integrity community rather than a policy instrument. It complements: UNESCO 2023 GenAI guidance (supportive regulation rather than prohibition), ICAI 2021 update (applying 6 values to AI contexts), and Perkins' AIAS framework (tiered AI permission levels). The postplagiarism concept provides the normative rationale for why policy frameworks like AIAS are necessary.

**Pháp luật Việt Nam:**

> Vietnam's Decree 109/2022 Dieu 20 mandates academic integrity codes but defines plagiarism in traditional terms (text copying) with no reference to AI-generated content. The postplagiarism framework is directly applicable to the gap in Vietnamese law: the responsibility principle (Tenet 4) and attribution principle (Tenet 5) could inform updated Decree language. As of 2024-2025, Vietnam's regulatory framework has not incorporated postplagiarism concepts.

**Chính sách châu Á:**

> CUHK's 2023 expulsions for AI use, NUS's disclosure framework, and NTU's zero-marks policy for hallucinated citations can all be analyzed through Eaton's tenets: CUHK enforced Tenet 4 (responsibility cannot be outsourced); NUS operationalized Tenet 5 (attribution); NTU applied Tenet 6 (rethinking what plagiarism means when AI fabricates references). Asian institutions are moving from prohibition toward responsibility-based frameworks consistent with postplagiarism.

### Góc nhìn EdTech

**AI tutor vs AI cheater:**

> Eaton's postplagiarism framework provides a principled resolution to the tutor/cheater debate: AI is neither categorically a tutor nor a cheater — the determining factor is the human's exercise of responsibility. When students engage critically with AI output, document their cognitive contributions, and attribute appropriately, AI functions as a legitimate intellectual tool. When students displace responsibility and misrepresent AI output as their own competence, it is cheating regardless of technical detection.

**Chính sách EdTech companies:**

> Khan Academy Khanmigo (Socratic method, refuses essay completion) embodies Tenet 4 — it requires students to retain cognitive responsibility. Coursera's post-submission dialogue approach operationalizes attribution (Tenet 5) by creating a record of human-AI interaction. The postplagiarism framework provides the theoretical basis for designing EdTech tools that support responsible AI use rather than either enabling fraud or prohibiting AI entirely.

**Đề xuất nhà nghiên cứu EdTech:**

> From an EdTech researcher perspective applying Eaton's postplagiarism framework: (1) Replace plagiarism detection with attribution verification systems; (2) Redesign assessments to demonstrate process, not just output; (3) Develop AI literacy curricula grounded in responsibility and attribution principles; (4) Update institutional policies to shift from 'AI prohibited/permitted' to 'AI disclosed/undisclosed'; (5) Apply postplagiarism tenets to Vietnam's regulatory gap — use Tenets 4-5 to draft AI attribution standards for Decree 109 updates.

### Nguồn tài liệu

**Bài báo khoa học:**

> Eaton, S.E. (2023). Postplagiarism: A conceptual framework for understanding academic integrity in an age of artificial intelligence. International Journal for Educational Integrity, DOI: 10.1007/s40979-023-00144-1. Eaton, S.E. & Christensen Hughes, J. (eds.) (2022). Academic Integrity in Canada. Springer. Perkins, M. et al. (2023). Academic Integrity Considerations of AI Large Language Models in the Post-Pandemic Era. Journal of University Teaching & Learning Practice.

**Sách tham khảo:**

> Eaton, S.E. (2021). Plagiarism in Higher Education: Tackling Tough Topics in Academic Integrity. ABC-CLIO. Eaton, S.E. (ed.) (2024). Second Handbook of Academic Integrity. Springer. Bretag, T. (2016). Handbook of Academic Integrity. Springer. ICAI (2021). The Fundamental Values of Academic Integrity, 3rd ed.

**Journal:** International Journal for Educational Integrity
