## Rose Luckin {#rose-luckin}

| Field | Value |
|---|---|
| Type | researcher |
| Institution | University College London (UCL), Institute of Education — Professor Emerita; Founder and CEO, Educate Ventures Research (EVR) |
| Institution | University College London (UCL), Institute of Education — Professor Emerita; Founder and CEO, Educate Ventures Research (EVR) |
| Specialty | AI as personal tutor; intelligence augmentation; democratizing education through AI; foundational EdTech optimist |

### Định nghĩa & Khái niệm

**Định nghĩa:**

> Luckin defines AI in education (AIEd) as the application of AI techniques — including machine learning, natural language processing, and adaptive systems — to support learning. Central to her framework is 'intelligence augmentation': AI should enhance and extend human intelligence rather than replace it.

**Phân loại vi phạm:**

> Luckin's work is not primarily focused on misconduct classification. She frames AI in terms of use modes: AI as personal tutor (one-on-one adaptive instruction), AI as learning partner (collaborative problem-solving), and AI as intelligence augmentation (metacognitive support). Misuse of AI represents a failure of AI literacy and institutional design, not a fundamental property of the technology.

**Lịch sử khái niệm:**

> Luckin has been developing and writing about AIEd for over 30 years. 'Intelligence Unleashed' (2016, Pearson/UCL Knowledge Lab), co-authored with Holmes, Griffiths, and Corcier, became the foundational optimist text in EdTech — arguing AI could democratize access to personalized, expert-quality tutoring at global scale.

### Bằng chứng thực tế

**Tên trường hợp:**

> Intelligence Unleashed (Pearson 2016): AI as one-on-one tutor; UCL Knowledge Lab AIEd research (ongoing); Educate Ventures Research (EVR) — consultancy applying AIEd ethically; Luckin's keynote and policy contributions worldwide including UNESCO AI in Education consultations.

**Diễn biến:**

> Luckin argues that quality education has historically been constrained by teacher-student ratios — only wealthy students could access personalized one-on-one tutoring (the 'two sigma problem' identified by Bloom). AI tutors could provide this personalized instruction at scale, democratizing access for all students regardless of economic background. Intelligence Unleashed set out the vision and early technical possibilities. Subsequent work refined this around ethical AI deployment and teacher empowerment.

**Kết quả xử lý:**

> Luckin's work influenced UNESCO AI in Education policy, UK government EdTech strategies, and hundreds of EdTech startups. EVR provides practical consultancy helping educational institutions leverage AI ethically. Her research has shaped the pedagogically optimistic strand of AIEd discourse globally.

**Con số thống kê:**

> Luckin has over 30 years of research in AIEd. Intelligence Unleashed (2016) was downloaded and cited thousands of times and is one of the most influential AIEd policy documents. Google Scholar shows hundreds of citations for her key papers. EVR works with institutions across multiple continents.

### Tác hại & Hậu quả

**Hại cho người học:**

> From Luckin's optimist perspective, the harm to learners comes not from AI itself but from misuse or exclusion. Students who lack access to AI tutoring are disadvantaged; students who misuse AI for fraud deprive themselves of genuine learning. Her framing emphasizes AI's capacity to support metacognition and self-regulated learning.

**Hại cho nghiên cứu:**

> Luckin acknowledges that AI systems trained on biased data can perpetuate educational inequalities. Poor AIEd implementations can create false signals about student competency. Her ethical AI framework addresses these risks proactively.

**Hại cho xã hội:**

> Luckin warns that without equitable access, AI in education will widen the digital divide — high-income students will benefit from AI tutoring while others are excluded. Her democratization argument is simultaneously a warning about the harms of unequal deployment.

### Phát hiện & Công cụ

**Công cụ phát hiện:**

> Luckin's work is not focused on fraud detection tools. She argues that if AI is properly integrated as a learning tool with appropriate attribution, the detection problem diminishes — students use AI openly rather than covertly.

**Hạn chế công cụ:**

> Luckin implicitly acknowledges that surveillance-based detection is counterproductive — it creates adversarial dynamics that undermine the trust necessary for genuine learning. Her preferred solution is institutional design that makes AI use transparent and attributed.

### Phòng ngừa & Giải pháp

**Thiết kế bài tập:**

> Luckin's AIEd vision implies assessment should adapt to AI-augmented learning — not simply testing knowledge recall but assessing higher-order thinking, creativity, and metacognition that AI cannot substitute for. She supports portfolio, project-based, and dialogic assessment formats.

**Chính sách trường học:**

> Luckin advocates for institutional policies that embrace AI as a learning tool with clear ethical guidelines, rather than blanket prohibition. She argues that banning AI creates underground use without guidance, whereas integrating it with appropriate scaffolding builds genuine competency.

**Văn hóa tổ chức:**

> Luckin's approach implies a trust-based institutional culture where AI use is disclosed, discussed, and evaluated pedagogically. She supports teacher empowerment — giving educators the knowledge and tools to use AI effectively — rather than top-down surveillance.

**AI Literacy:**

> AI literacy is central to Luckin's framework. She argues educators and students must understand AI's capabilities and limitations, which is the foundation for both effective use and ethical use. EVR's work is substantially focused on AI literacy professional development.

### Khung pháp lý & Đạo đức

**Khung quốc tế:**

> Luckin has contributed to UNESCO AI in Education policy documents and advised the UK government. Her Intelligence Unleashed framework has influenced OECD and EU discussions on AIEd. She co-wrote AI guidance for UNESCO.

### Góc nhìn EdTech

**AI tutor vs AI cheater:**

> Luckin is the canonical voice on 'AI as tutor.' Intelligence Unleashed (2016) establishes the foundational argument that AI can serve as a personal tutor for every student, democratizing quality education. This stands in contrast to the 'AI as cheater' framing dominant in integrity literature. Luckin argues both are real phenomena, but the tutoring potential is larger and more transformative.

**Chính sách EdTech companies:**

> Through EVR, Luckin advises EdTech companies on ethical AI integration. She engages with platforms like Google for Education, and her frameworks influence how companies like Khan Academy design their AI tutoring features (Khanmigo's Socratic approach reflects Luckin-style principles).

**Đề xuất nhà nghiên cứu EdTech:**

> Luckin recommends: (1) reframe AI as intelligence augmentation rather than threat; (2) invest in AI literacy for teachers and students; (3) design educational AI tools that make learning processes visible and support metacognition; (4) deploy AI equitably — address the digital divide in AI access; (5) establish ethical AI deployment standards that put learner wellbeing first; (6) use AI to address the 'two sigma problem' at scale.

### Nguồn tài liệu

**Bài báo khoa học:**

> Luckin et al. (2016) Intelligence Unleashed (Pearson/UCL); multiple papers in International Journal of Artificial Intelligence in Education (IJAIED); contributions to UNESCO AI in Education guidance documents; over 200 publications in AIEd across 30+ years.

**Sách tham khảo:**

> Luckin, R., Holmes, W., Griffiths, M., & Corcier, L.B. (2016). Intelligence Unleashed: An Argument for AI in Education. London: Pearson. Available free at UCL Discovery and Pearson Open Ideas. This is the foundational optimist EdTech text on AI tutoring.

## Donald L. McCabe {#donald-l-mccabe}

| Field | Value |
|---|---|
| Type | researcher |
| Institution | Rutgers Business School, Rutgers University (Newark and New Brunswick, NJ, USA); Co-founder, International Center for Academic Integrity (ICAI) |
| Institution | Rutgers Business School, Rutgers University (Newark and New Brunswick, NJ, USA); Co-founder, International Center for Academic Integrity (ICAI) |

### Định nghĩa & Khái niệm

**Định nghĩa:**

> Donald L. McCabe is widely regarded as the 'founding father' or 'grandfather' of empirical academic integrity research. His definition of academic integrity, operationalized through the ICAI, centers on six fundamental values: honesty, trust, fairness, respect, responsibility, and courage — values that institutions must model and students must internalize.

**Phân loại vi phạm:**

> McCabe's research taxonomy of academic dishonesty covers: cheating on tests/exams, plagiarism, unauthorized collaboration, fabrication, facilitation (helping others cheat), and falsification. His framework predates the AI era but remains the foundational classification system extended by AI-era researchers including Eaton and Perkins.

**Lịch sử khái niệm:**

> McCabe began foundational academic integrity research in the early 1990s, building on Bowers' 1964 survey. In 1993, McCabe and Treviño published the most consequential student misconduct survey to that point. McCabe co-founded ICAI (International Center for Academic Integrity) in 1992, transforming what had been isolated institutional practices into a recognized academic field. He conducted surveys of 70,000+ students across hundreds of U.S. and Canadian institutions over 30 years.

### Bằng chứng thực tế

**Tên trường hợp:**

> Not applicable — this is a researcher profile, not a case. McCabe's research has informed responses to cases of academic dishonesty worldwide, including the Texas A&M 2023 false positive case and the broader institutional policy debates triggered by AI writing tools.

**Diễn biến:** Not applicable — researcher profile.

**Kết quả xử lý:**

> Not applicable — researcher profile. McCabe's legacy: when he retired from Rutgers, he transferred stewardship of his longitudinal survey instrument to ICAI, which continues the McCabe-ICAI Academic Integrity Survey. His research directly influenced honor code adoption at hundreds of institutions.

**Con số thống kê:**

> 70,000+ undergraduate and graduate students surveyed across U.S. and Canadian institutions (career total). Over 100 institutions participated in McCabe's surveys. Key finding: approximately 2/3 (64%) of surveyed college students self-reported at least one form of cheating. 95% of surveyed high school students reported participating in some form of cheating. Students at honor code institutions view academic integrity fundamentally differently from non-honor code students.

### Tác hại & Hậu quả

**Hại cho người học:**

> McCabe's research documented the harm to individual learners: students who cheat fail to develop genuine competencies, create dependency on dishonest shortcuts, and suffer long-term career disadvantage when real skills are required. His honor code research showed that culture-based integrity education produces more lasting behavioral change than punitive enforcement alone.

**Hại cho nghiên cứu:**

> McCabe's survey methodology (self-report, longitudinal, multi-institutional) established the evidentiary foundation for academic integrity as a field. His finding that 2/3 of students self-report cheating provided the empirical basis for treating academic dishonesty as a systemic rather than individual problem.

**Hại cho xã hội:**

> McCabe's research documented that academic dishonesty erodes the value of educational credentials, reduces trust in institutional certification, and produces graduates who may carry dishonest habits into professional contexts. The societal cost of integrity failures in education — healthcare professionals, engineers, lawyers who cheated through school — motivated his life's work.

### Phát hiện & Công cụ

**Công cụ phát hiện:**

> McCabe's methodology was primarily survey-based (self-report of misconduct), not detection-tool based. He argued that understanding student motivations and cultural context was more important than detecting individual violations. His approach influenced the shift from detection-first to culture-first integrity policy in honor code institutions.

**Hạn chế công cụ:**

> McCabe was skeptical of purely enforcement-based approaches. His research consistently showed that punitive detection systems without underlying culture change (honor codes, community standards) produced minimal long-term behavioral improvement. This critique is directly applicable to AI detection tool debates in the 2020s.

**LLM Watermarking:**

> McCabe's work predates LLM watermarking. However, his foundational insight — that technology-based detection alone is insufficient without cultural norm formation — is directly relevant to watermarking policy debates. Watermarking addresses provenance but not motivation; McCabe's research addresses motivation.

### Phòng ngừa & Giải pháp

**Thiết kế bài tập:**

> McCabe's honor code research informed assignment design principles: creating learning environments where students feel personally invested in integrity reduces cheating more than surveillance-based assessment design. Oral defenses, portfolio-based assessment, and community accountability align with McCabe's cultural approach.

**Chính sách trường học:**

> McCabe's most policy-influential finding: modified honor codes — where students pledge integrity AND faculty treat students as trusted partners AND institutional norms consistently model integrity — reduce cheating rates more effectively than traditional honor codes or enforcement-only policies. This finding shaped honor code reform at Stanford, Duke, MIT, and hundreds of other institutions.

**Văn hóa tổ chức:**

> McCabe's central contribution: academic integrity is a cultural phenomenon, not primarily an enforcement problem. The 'culture of integrity' framework (versus 'culture of compliance') is his most cited conceptual contribution. Institutions with strong honor code cultures show 25-30% lower self-reported cheating rates than comparable institutions without such cultures.

**AI Literacy:**

> McCabe's framework, extended to the AI era, suggests that AI literacy must include internalized ethical norms about AI use — not just rules about what is permitted or detectable. Students must understand why disclosure and attribution matter, not just which tools are prohibited.

### Khung pháp lý & Đạo đức

**Khung quốc tế:**

> ICAI (which McCabe co-founded) contributes to UNESCO, EU, and international academic integrity frameworks. The ICAI Fundamental Values of Academic Integrity — honesty, trust, fairness, respect, responsibility, courage — are referenced in international policy documents on academic integrity.

**Pháp luật Việt Nam:**

> McCabe's framework influenced Nghị định 109/2022/NĐ-CP's emphasis on research integrity culture alongside enforcement. Vietnamese higher education reform documents (including QĐ 1705/QĐ-TTg) cite international best practices in academic integrity that are rooted in McCabe's empirical foundation.

**Chính sách châu Á:**

> ICAI has expanded internationally, including Asian member institutions. NUS (Singapore), CUHK (Hong Kong), and universities in Japan and South Korea have adopted honor code frameworks influenced by McCabe's research. The McCabe-ICAI survey instrument has been adapted for Asian institutional contexts.

### Góc nhìn EdTech

**AI tutor vs AI cheater:**

> McCabe's cultural integrity framework is the key to resolving the AI tutor vs. AI cheater tension: institutions that build genuine cultures of integrity — where students understand why they are learning and value authentic competence — are less vulnerable to AI-as-cheater dynamics. Detection-first approaches (without culture) will always lose the arms race against AI writing tools.

**Chính sách EdTech companies:**

> McCabe's research has been cited by Turnitin, Coursera, and Khan Academy in developing academic integrity policies. His finding that trust-based environments reduce cheating has influenced Khan Academy's Khanmigo design (Socratic dialogue, refuses to write essays for students) over pure detection.

**Đề xuất nhà nghiên cứu EdTech:**

> McCabe's most policy-relevant contributions for EdTech researchers: (1) Build honor code cultures, not just enforcement systems — culture predicts integrity better than surveillance; (2) Engage students as partners in integrity, not suspects; (3) Faculty modeling of integrity behaviors is as important as student enforcement; (4) Survey-based research (self-report) provides access to dishonesty that detection tools cannot; (5) Institutional norms must be consistent across all contexts — policies that are applied selectively undermine trust.

### Nguồn tài liệu

**Bài báo khoa học:**

> McCabe, D.L., & Treviño, L.K. (1993). 'Academic dishonesty: Honor codes and other contextual influences.' Journal of Higher Education, 64(5), 522-538. McCabe, D.L., Treviño, L.K., & Butterfield, K.D. (2001). 'Cheating in Academic Institutions: A Decade of Research.' Ethics & Behavior, 11(3), 219-232. Multiple Springer Nature chapter contributions to academic integrity handbooks.

**Sách tham khảo:**

> McCabe, D.L., Butterfield, K.D., & Treviño, L.K. (2012). Cheating in College: Why Students Do It and What Educators Can Do About It. Baltimore: Johns Hopkins University Press. (Key finding: 2/3 self-report cheating; honor code culture most effective deterrent; surveyed 70,000+ students.)

**Nguồn Việt Nam:**

> McCabe's work is cited in Vietnamese academic integrity literature. Nghị định 109/2022/NĐ-CP and MOET guidance documents reference international integrity frameworks derived from ICAI/McCabe. No Vietnamese translation of 'Cheating in College' is confirmed, but the framework is taught in Vietnamese education research graduate programs.

### Uncertain Fields

- year (birth/death dates not researched for this profile — McCabe passed away in 2021 [uncertain — requires verification])
- con_so_thong_ke (70,000 figure cited varies by source — some sources say 70,000 undergrad, others say 100,000+ total including high school surveys)

## Phil Newton {#phil-newton}

| Field | Value |
|---|---|
| Type | researcher |
| Institution | Swansea University Medical School (Professor) |
| Institution | Swansea University Medical School (Professor) |
| Specialty | Contract cheating prevalence; ChatGPT threat to assessment; evidence-based education research |

### Định nghĩa & Khái niệm

**Định nghĩa:**

> Newton's work operationalizes contract cheating as any instance where a student submits work completed by another person or AI system as their own. His systematic reviews provide empirical prevalence estimates rather than definitional elaborations, contributing to evidence-based policy-making.

**Phân loại vi phạm:**

> Newton's research addresses commercial contract cheating (essay mills), file-sharing platforms, and — from 2022 onward — AI tools (particularly ChatGPT) as a new category of academic misconduct that blurs the boundary between tool use and outsourcing.

**Lịch sử khái niệm:**

> Newton's 2018 Frontiers in Education systematic review was landmark in establishing evidence-based prevalence estimates for contract cheating. His subsequent work on ChatGPT (2023) extends the research to the AI era, documenting how LLMs qualitatively change the threat landscape.

### Bằng chứng thực tế

**Tên trường hợp:**

> Newton (2018): 'How Common Is Commercial Contract Cheating in Higher Education and Is It Increasing? A Systematic Review' — Frontiers in Education; Newton & Xiromeriti (2023): 'ChatGPT and Higher Education Assessments' — Computers & Education: Artificial Intelligence, doi:10.1016/j.caeai.2023.100114.

**Diễn biến:**

> Newton's 2018 systematic review of the literature found that across samples from 2014 onward, 15.7% of students self-reported having paid someone else to complete academic work — potentially representing 31 million students globally. The 2023 paper with Xiromeriti analyzed ChatGPT's performance on multiple-choice assessments, finding it could pass assessments in many disciplines, fundamentally threatening traditional assessment validity.

**Kết quả xử lý:**

> Newton's research has informed UK legislative efforts to ban essay mills. His ChatGPT paper (2023) has been widely cited in institutional policy reviews. Newton advocates for assessment redesign rather than detection-only approaches.

**Con số thống kê:**

> 15.7% contract cheating self-report rate from systematic review (2018), potentially 31 million students globally. ChatGPT performance analysis (2023): passed assessments in multiple academic disciplines including medicine and law. Newton's papers have thousands of citations in academic integrity literature.

### Tác hại & Hậu quả

**Hại cho người học:**

> Students who contract-cheat bypass the learning process, acquiring credentials without developing the knowledge and skills the credential is meant to certify. Newton's research shows this is not marginal but affects a significant share of the student population, creating a systemic credentialing problem.

**Hại cho nghiên cứu:**

> Newton's medical school context makes the stakes concrete: a medical student who passes exams via ChatGPT or essay mills has not demonstrated clinical reasoning ability. The falsity of their credential has direct patient safety implications.

**Hại cho xã hội:**

> Newton's 31-million estimate illustrates the scale of credential inflation when contract cheating goes unchecked. Professional licensing bodies and employers cannot rely on degrees as competency signals if cheating is this widespread.

### Phát hiện & Công cụ

**Công cụ phát hiện:**

> Newton's research examines detection challenges rather than specific tool advocacy. His ChatGPT paper implicitly shows that traditional plagiarism detection (Turnitin) cannot catch AI-generated content, since AI text is original in the technical sense.

**Hạn chế công cụ:**

> The central finding of Newton & Xiromeriti (2023) is that ChatGPT can pass assessments without triggering conventional detection — because AI-generated text is not plagiarized from prior sources. This represents a fundamental limitation of current detection infrastructure.

### Phòng ngừa & Giải pháp

**Thiết kế bài tập:**

> Newton implies that multiple-choice and essay assessments that ChatGPT can pass must be redesigned. He implicitly endorses process-based, oral, and contextualized assessment formats that require genuine student engagement.

**Chính sách trường học:**

> Newton's research has been cited in UK policy discussions about essay mill legislation and in HE institution responses to ChatGPT. He does not himself prescribe specific institutional models but his data underpins policy urgency.

**Văn hóa tổ chức:**

> Newton's work focuses more on prevalence and systemic risk than on cultural solutions. His research suggests that current honor-code and detection approaches are insufficient given the scale of the problem.

### Khung pháp lý & Đạo đức

**Khung quốc tế:**

> Newton's systematic review methodology and ChatGPT paper have been cited across international contexts. His work contributes to the evidence base informing ICAI, Transparency International, and national HE regulatory bodies.

### Góc nhìn EdTech

**AI tutor vs AI cheater:**

> Newton's work firmly establishes the AI-as-cheater threat. His ChatGPT paper demonstrates that AI can substitute for student learning in assessed contexts, making it a direct enabler of academic fraud rather than a pedagogical tool in these use cases.

**Đề xuất nhà nghiên cứu EdTech:**

> Newton recommends: (1) urgent recognition that ChatGPT fundamentally changes the assessment threat landscape; (2) systematic redesign of assessments that AI can easily pass; (3) policy frameworks that address both essay mills and AI tools; (4) legislative action against commercial contract cheating services; (5) evidence-based institutional responses rather than ad hoc bans.

### Nguồn tài liệu

**Bài báo khoa học:**

> Newton (2018) Frontiers in Education — systematic review, 15.7% contract cheating rate; Newton & Xiromeriti (2023) Computers & Education: Artificial Intelligence, doi:10.1016/j.caeai.2023.100114 — ChatGPT threat to assessments; additional publications on contract cheating prevalence and international predictors.

**Sách tham khảo:**

> Newton has contributed to Bretag (ed.) Handbook of Academic Integrity (Springer 2016) and related edited volumes. His research is frequently cited in Eaton (2021/2024) work on AI and academic integrity.

## Mike Perkins {#mike-perkins}

| Field | Value |
|---|---|
| Type | researcher |
| Institution | British University Vietnam (BUV), Hanoi — Head of Quality and Academic Development / Centre for Research & Innovation |
| Institution | British University Vietnam (BUV), Hanoi — Head of Quality and Academic Development / Centre for Research & Innovation |
| Specialty | AI detection in higher education; AIAS 5-tier framework; GenAI ethical integration; most directly relevant to Vietnamese HE context |

### Định nghĩa & Khái niệm

**Định nghĩa:**

> Perkins defines the AI integration challenge not primarily as a cheating problem but as an assessment design problem. His AIAS framework provides a 5-level operational definition of appropriate AI use — from 'no AI' to 'full AI integration' — allowing educators to communicate clear, context-specific expectations.

**Phân loại vi phạm:**

> Perkins distinguishes between: AI fraud (submitting fully AI-generated work without disclosure), AI-assisted misconduct (using AI in violation of task-specific rules), and legitimate AI use (transparent, disclosed, appropriately bounded AI assistance). His AIAS framework maps these distinctions onto a practical 5-tier scale.

**Lịch sử khái niệm:**

> Perkins' 'Chatting and Cheating' paper (IETI 2023) was among the first peer-reviewed studies to empirically examine ChatGPT's impact on academic integrity in higher education. The AIAS framework (introduced 2023, updated v2 2024) emerged as a positive, forward-looking response: instead of banning AI, design assessments with explicit AI-use levels.

### Bằng chứng thực tế

**Tên trường hợp:**

> Perkins et al. (2023): 'Chatting and Cheating: Ensuring Academic Integrity in the Era of ChatGPT' — Innovations in Education and Teaching International (IETI), doi:10.1080/14703297.2023.2190148; Perkins et al. (2024): Detection of GPT-4 in academic writing — Journal of Academic Ethics; AIAS Framework (2023/2024); pilot implementation study in Australasian Journal of Educational Technology.

**Diễn biến:**

> Perkins' 2023 IETI paper analyzed how ChatGPT could be used to generate plausible academic submissions and what this means for institutional integrity. His team at BUV developed the AIAS (Artificial Intelligence Assessment Scale) as a practical tool for educators to classify assignments by permitted AI use level. The AIAS pilot showed significant reduction in AI-related misconduct cases. By 2024, the AIAS had been adopted by hundreds of institutions worldwide, translated into 30+ languages, and recognized by TEQSA (Australia).

**Kết quả xử lý:**

> The AIAS framework has become an internationally recognized standard. TEQSA cited it in 2024 as an option for implementing GenAI in assessment. BUV's pilot found reduced misconduct cases and improved student engagement with AI tools. Perkins' work demonstrates that constructive frameworks outperform prohibition strategies.

**Con số thống kê:**

> AIAS adopted by hundreds of institutions globally; translated into 30+ languages; recognized by TEQSA 2024. Perkins' 'Chatting and Cheating' paper is among the most-cited early papers on ChatGPT and academic integrity (2023). BUV pilot: measurable reduction in GenAI-related misconduct cases.

### Tác hại & Hậu quả

**Hại cho người học:**

> Perkins frames AI fraud as harmful because it deprives students of genuine learning. His AIAS solution addresses this positively — by designing assessments where AI use is bounded and transparent, students develop both subject competency and AI literacy simultaneously.

**Hại cho nghiên cứu:**

> Perkins' detection research shows that undetected AI submissions corrupt assessment data, undermining evidence-based pedagogy. His work on GPT-4 detection (2024) documents the reliability limits of current AI detectors.

**Hại cho xã hội:**

> Perkins notes that in the Vietnamese and Southeast Asian context, international credentialing pressure creates particular vulnerability to AI fraud — students seeking to signal competency for global employers may be tempted to use AI dishonestly when assessment design does not clearly permit or prohibit it.

### Phát hiện & Công cụ

**Công cụ phát hiện:**

> Perkins' 2024 paper examines the detection of GPT-4-generated text in academic writing, evaluating tools including Turnitin AI Detection, GPTZero, and Copyleaks. He finds that detection accuracy is imperfect, with meaningful false-positive rates, and argues that detection should complement — not replace — assessment redesign.

**Hạn chế công cụ:**

> Perkins documents that AI detectors fail on: (1) paraphrased/humanized AI text; (2) non-native English writers (elevated false positive rates); (3) GPT-4 compared to GPT-3.5. His work supports Liang et al. (Stanford 2023) findings on ESL false positives. He concludes detection tools are unreliable as sole integrity mechanisms.

### Phòng ngừa & Giải pháp

**Thiết kế bài tập:**

> The AIAS framework is fundamentally a tool for assessment redesign. Level 1 (No AI) requires complete human work; Level 5 (Full AI Integration) allows AI throughout with human oversight and attribution. The framework enables educators to design assessments appropriate to their learning objectives while being transparent with students.

**Chính sách trường học:**

> Perkins' AIAS is the most actionable institutional policy framework in the current literature. It has been adopted by institutions in Australia, Vietnam, UK, and dozens of other countries. BUV's implementation demonstrates the framework's effectiveness in a Vietnamese HE context — making it uniquely relevant for Vietnamese institutions.

**Văn hóa tổ chức:**

> Perkins argues for a culture of transparent AI use with clear attribution standards, rather than prohibition or adversarial detection. His AIAS promotes institutional trust by giving students clear guidance rather than ambiguous rules.

**AI Literacy:**

> Perkins' AIAS framework explicitly builds AI literacy by requiring students to understand and attribute AI contributions. His work aligns with UNESCO's AI literacy mandates and provides a practical curriculum-level implementation pathway.

### Khung pháp lý & Đạo đức

**Khung quốc tế:**

> Perkins engages with TEQSA (Australia), UNESCO AI in Education guidance, and international EdTech policy. His AIAS framework is recognized across multiple national regulatory contexts. The 30+ language translations reflect global adoption.

**Pháp luật Việt Nam:**

> Perkins works at BUV in Hanoi, making his research directly relevant to Vietnam. While not focused on Nghị định 109/2022 or Luật KH&CN 2025, his AIAS framework addresses the exact gap in Vietnamese law — no explicit governance of AI-generated content in academic submissions. BUV's pilot provides a local evidence base for Vietnamese institutions considering AIAS adoption.

**Chính sách châu Á:**

> Perkins' BUV context (Hanoi, Vietnam) provides direct Southeast Asian application. His AIAS framework has been adopted by Asian universities and is more operationally specific than CUHK or NUS policy frameworks. The BUV pilot is a documented Asian case study of constructive AI integration.

### Góc nhìn EdTech

**AI tutor vs AI cheater:**

> Perkins occupies a middle position in the Luckin vs. Selwyn debate — he acknowledges both AI's tutoring potential and its fraud risk, and argues the solution is not to choose sides but to design clear boundaries (AIAS) that enable AI as a learning tool while preventing fraud.

**Chính sách EdTech companies:**

> Perkins' AIAS framework is being evaluated for integration with EdTech platforms. His BUV context involves working with Coursera and similar platforms. The framework provides a common language for EdTech companies to communicate their AI integration policies to educators.

**Đề xuất nhà nghiên cứu EdTech:**

> Perkins recommends: (1) adopt the AIAS framework institutionally rather than blanket AI bans; (2) design assessments that explicitly state permitted AI use level; (3) require attribution for all AI contributions; (4) use detection tools as one layer in a multi-pronged approach; (5) build AI literacy into curricula; (6) establish clear institutional policies before students encounter AI in assessments. Especially relevant: implement AIAS at Vietnamese universities to address the governance gap in current Vietnamese law.

### Nguồn tài liệu

**Bài báo khoa học:**

> Perkins et al. (2023) IETI doi:10.1080/14703297.2023.2190148 — 'Chatting and Cheating'; Perkins et al. (2024) Journal of Academic Ethics — Detection of GPT-4; AIAS framework paper in Journal of University Teaching and Learning Practice; pilot study in Australasian Journal of Educational Technology (AJET).

**Sách tham khảo:**

> AIAS Framework documentation (aiassessmentscale.com); Perkins' research profile at ResearchGate (British University Vietnam); contributions to Springer literature on AI in higher education.

**Nguồn Việt Nam:**

> Perkins' research is directly conducted at British University Vietnam (BUV), Hanoi. BUV's AIAS pilot represents one of the few documented empirical studies of AI integrity intervention conducted in a Vietnamese educational institution. This makes his work uniquely valuable for Vietnamese EdTech research.

## Neil Selwyn {#neil-selwyn}

| Field | Value |
|---|---|
| Type | researcher |
| Institution | Monash University — Faculty of Arts (Professor, School of Education) |
| Institution | Monash University — Faculty of Arts (Professor, School of Education) |
| Specialty | Critical sociology of EdTech; AI cheating as systemic symptom of credential pressure and inequality; critical limits of AI in education |

### Định nghĩa & Khái niệm

**Định nghĩa:**

> Selwyn does not offer a conventional academic integrity definition. Instead, he frames cheating as a socially produced behavior — a rational response to institutional pressures (credential demands, inequality, inadequate assessment design) rather than primarily a moral failing. This critical sociological lens reframes 'what is academic misconduct' as 'what structural conditions produce misconduct.'

**Phân loại vi phạm:**

> Selwyn's work cuts across standard misconduct categories. He argues that distinctions between 'legitimate' AI use and 'cheating' are socially constructed and shaped by power relations. The categories themselves reflect institutional interests and may not align with what constitutes meaningful learning.

**Lịch sử khái niệm:**

> Selwyn has worked for 30 years on critical digital education research. His recent work on AI and education (2022-2025) applies his longstanding critical sociological method to AI — examining not just what AI can do but who benefits, who is harmed, and what structural conditions shape AI adoption in education.

### Bằng chứng thực tế

**Tên trường hợp:**

> Selwyn (2022): 'The Future of AI and Education: Some Cautionary Notes' — European Journal of Education; Selwyn (2024): 'On the Limits of Artificial Intelligence (AI) in Education' — Monash University publication; multiple Monash University research projects on AI, smartphone bans, digital reading, and educational work.

**Diễn biến:**

> Selwyn argues that cheating driven by AI tools is primarily a symptom of deeper structural problems: (1) credential inflation — students need degrees to access work, creating high-stakes pressure; (2) inequality — students from disadvantaged backgrounds face additional pressures and fewer resources; (3) assessment design failure — if an assessment can be passed by ChatGPT, it was not testing genuine competency. Selwyn argues that surveillance-based detection responses ('AI cop') are counterproductive — they create adversarial environments that damage trust and learning culture.

**Kết quả xử lý:**

> Selwyn's critical framework has influenced EdTech researchers and policymakers who recognize that technical detection alone cannot solve structurally driven cheating. His 'cautionary notes' on AI have been cited widely as a counterbalance to uncritical techno-optimism.

**Con số thống kê:**

> Selwyn's work is qualitative and structural rather than quantitative. His research projects at Monash involve ethnographic and sociological methods. He has published 30+ years of research cited thousands of times in digital education literature.

### Tác hại & Hậu quả

**Hại cho người học:**

> Selwyn argues that uncritical AI adoption can harm learners by: reorganizing education around 'machine-readable' metrics that reduce the richness of human learning; perpetuating biases that disadvantage minoritized students; creating dependency on AI tools that atrophies critical thinking. Both AI fraud and naive AI integration harm learners.

**Hại cho nghiên cứu:**

> Selwyn's work critiques AI systems that perpetuate statistical models of learning — losing the complexity of human cognition and education in favor of data-optimizable metrics. This reductionism harms the integrity of educational research itself.

**Hại cho xã hội:**

> Selwyn identifies ecological costs (data and energy intensity of AI), social costs (perpetuating inequality through unequal AI access), and cultural costs (reorganizing education to serve economic productivity rather than human flourishing) as systemic harms from uncritical AI in education.

### Phát hiện & Công cụ

**Công cụ phát hiện:**

> Selwyn is critical of surveillance-based detection as a primary response to AI cheating. He argues that AI detectors create adversarial dynamics, produce false positives that harm innocent students (especially ESL writers), and do not address root causes. Detection may make students more sophisticated at evasion rather than less likely to cheat.

**Hạn chế công cụ:**

> Selwyn's sociological critique is one of the strongest arguments against detection-first approaches. He argues detection treats a structural problem as an individual behavior problem — like treating poverty by fining poor people. The arms race between detection and humanization tools produces no durable solution.

### Phòng ngừa & Giải pháp

**Thiết kế bài tập:**

> Selwyn supports assessment redesign but frames it sociologically — the goal is not just to design AI-resistant assessments but to design assessments that serve genuine learning and reduce credentialing pressure. He implicitly supports process-based, dialogic, and portfolio assessments.

**Chính sách trường học:**

> Selwyn is skeptical of institutional policies that treat AI as primarily a compliance problem. He advocates for policies that address structural drivers: reduce credential pressure, improve equity of access to support, redesign assessment for genuine competency demonstration.

**Văn hóa tổ chức:**

> Selwyn's work strongly supports values-based, trust-oriented institutional culture (aligned with McCabe's honor code research) over enforcement-only approaches. He argues enforcement-only cultures produce adversarial dynamics that make integrity violations more, not less, likely.

**AI Literacy:**

> Selwyn supports critical AI literacy — not just technical understanding of AI, but sociological awareness of AI's power dynamics, biases, and structural effects. He argues students should be equipped to question AI systems, not just use them.

### Khung pháp lý & Đạo đức

**Khung quốc tế:**

> Selwyn's work engages with OECD, Australian, and European EdTech policy. His cautionary notes are cited in UNESCO and EU discussions alongside more optimistic frameworks. He represents the critical counterpoint in international AI-in-education policy debates.

### Góc nhìn EdTech

**AI tutor vs AI cheater:**

> Selwyn occupies the critical counter-position to both Luckin (AI as tutor) and conventional integrity researchers (AI as cheater). He argues that both framings are reductive — the real question is what structural conditions make AI use beneficial or harmful, and who has power to set those conditions. His work is the key 'AI cheating as systemic symptom' analysis in the debate.

**Chính sách EdTech companies:**

> Selwyn is critical of EdTech companies that prioritize commercial interests over pedagogical ones. He examines how companies like Google for Education, Duolingo, and Khan Academy shape educational practices in ways that serve platform growth rather than learner needs. He would critique Khanmigo's 'Socratic AI' as still embedding commercial assumptions about what learning is.

**Đề xuất nhà nghiên cứu EdTech:**

> Selwyn recommends: (1) address structural drivers of cheating — reduce credential pressure, improve equity; (2) critically examine who benefits from AI in education (often not students); (3) resist surveillance-based detection as primary response; (4) redesign assessments for genuine competency rather than AI-resistance; (5) build critical AI literacy that includes sociological awareness; (6) examine ecological and social costs of AI deployment before scaling.

### Nguồn tài liệu

**Bài báo khoa học:**

> Selwyn (2022) European Journal of Education — 'Future of AI and Education: Cautionary Notes'; Selwyn (2024) 'On the Limits of AI in Education'; multiple papers in Learning, Media and Technology; British Journal of Educational Technology; and Journal of Computer Assisted Learning spanning 30 years.

**Sách tham khảo:**

> Selwyn, N. — multiple books including 'Education and Technology' (Continuum); 'Distrusting Educational Technology' (Routledge); research monographs on digital education inequality. His work is the canonical critical EdTech reference.

## Edward Tian {#edward-tian}

| Field | Value |
|---|---|
| Type | researcher |
| Institution | Princeton University (Class of 2023, CS); CEO and co-founder of GPTZero Inc. |
| Institution | Princeton University (Class of 2023, CS); CEO and co-founder of GPTZero Inc. |
| Specialty | AI text detection; creator of GPTZero — the most widely used AI detector in education worldwide |

### Định nghĩa & Khái niệm

**Định nghĩa:**

> Tian's contribution is practical rather than definitional. GPTZero operationalizes AI-generated text detection through two measurable properties: perplexity (randomness/predictability of word choice within sentences) and burstiness (variation of perplexity across sentences). Human writing has high burstiness; AI text has low, uniform burstiness.

**Phân loại vi phạm:**

> Tian's work addresses a specific sub-category of AI-assisted misconduct: submitting AI-generated text as original human writing without disclosure. GPTZero does not distinguish between different categories of academic misconduct — it detects AI authorship probability, which educators then use to investigate potential integrity violations.

**Lịch sử khái niệm:**

> GPTZero was built over the 2022-2023 winter break (December-January) while Tian was a Princeton undergraduate. ChatGPT had launched November 30, 2022 and immediately generated concerns about AI-generated academic submissions. Tian released GPTZero on January 2, 2023. In week 1: 30,000 users; site crashed; Streamlit allocated emergency server capacity. By 2024: 2.5M+ educators using the platform.

### Bằng chứng thực tế

**Tên trường hợp:**

> GPTZero launch (Jan 2, 2023): 30,000 users in first week, site crashed; viral NPR story (Jan 9, 2023); May 2023: $3.5M seed funding; Summer 2024: $10M Series A; as of 2024: 2.5M+ educator users in 40+ countries; recognized as the leading AI detection tool for education.

**Diễn biến:**

> Tian, a Princeton CS student with a thesis on AI detection, spent winter break 2022-2023 building GPTZero in a Toronto coffee shop, motivated by concerns about ChatGPT being used to cheat on assignments. He tweeted the demo on January 2, 2023, and it went viral immediately — driven by educator anxiety about ChatGPT. The tool measures perplexity (sentence-level unpredictability) and burstiness (variation across sentences), exploiting the fact that AI text tends to be uniformly low-perplexity while human writing varies. GPTZero then evolved into a company with institutional clients, API access, and ongoing model improvements to track newer AI models.

**Kết quả xử lý:**

> GPTZero is now a commercial AI detection platform used by 2.5M+ educators. It has raised $13.5M total (seed + Series A). Tian graduated from Princeton in 2023 and became full-time CEO. GPTZero has faced criticism for false positives (especially ESL writers) and has invested in false-positive reduction features including a 'writing reports' feature providing detailed document analysis. The company added human writing verification features in response to false-positive concerns.

**Con số thống kê:**

> 30,000 users in first week (January 2023); site crashed; 40 US states and 30 countries in first months; 2.5M+ educators by 2024; $3.5M seed (May 2023); $10M Series A (Summer 2024); 40+ countries represented in user base; false positive rate: GPTZero has invested in reducing false positives but exact rates vary by model and writing style.

### Tác hại & Hậu quả

**Hại cho người học:**

> Students who submit AI-generated text without disclosure are not developing their writing, analytical, or critical thinking skills. Tian's motivation was explicitly to preserve the value of genuine student learning against AI shortcutting.

**Hại cho nghiên cứu:**

> AI-generated academic papers submitted without disclosure pollute the knowledge base with unverified, potentially hallucinated content. GPTZero's detection capability extends to research contexts — not just student assignments.

**Hại cho xã hội:**

> Undetected AI-generated submissions at scale devalue academic credentials, undermine employer trust in graduates, and — in professional training programs — create safety risks when graduate competency is not genuine.

### Phát hiện & Công cụ

**Công cụ phát hiện:**

> GPTZero is the primary tool created by Tian. Technical mechanism: (1) Perplexity — measures how surprised a language model is by each word in the text; AI text tends to be low-perplexity (predictable); human text is more varied. (2) Burstiness — humans write with varying sentence complexity (simple then complex); AI writes uniformly. (3) Later additions: paragraph-level highlighting, document-level scoring, humanized AI detection. Accuracy: strong on pure AI text; weaker on human-edited AI text.

**Hạn chế công cụ:**

> GPTZero's primary limitation is false positives, especially for: ESL/EFL writers (whose writing patterns may resemble AI); academic writing with formal style (naturally low perplexity); heavily edited text. Liang et al. (Stanford 2023) found 61.3% false positive rates for non-native English speakers using similar detection tools. GPTZero has publicly acknowledged this limitation and implemented mitigation features, but the arms race between detection and AI humanization tools continues.

**LLM Watermarking:**

> Tian is aware of LLM watermarking research (Kirchenbauer/UMD, Aaronson/OpenAI) as a complementary or alternative approach to post-hoc detection. GPTZero's detection-based approach differs from watermarking (which embeds signals in AI-generated text at generation time). Tian has noted that watermarking requires AI companies' cooperation and doesn't work for open-source models, supporting the continued need for external detection tools.

### Phòng ngừa & Giải pháp

**Thiết kế bài tập:**

> Tian's work implicitly supports assessment redesign — if assignments are designed so that AI cannot generate plausible responses (oral defenses, process portfolios, personalized prompts), detection becomes less necessary. GPTZero is a complement to, not a replacement for, good assessment design.

**Chính sách trường học:**

> Many institutions (K-12 and HE) have adopted GPTZero as part of their AI integrity policy toolkit. GPTZero provides institutional API access and integration with learning management systems. Tian has engaged with school districts and universities on policy frameworks for AI detection.

**Văn hóa tổ chức:**

> Tian has expressed concern about adversarial use of detection — institutions using GPTZero punitively without considering false positive risks. He advocates for detection as one tool in a broader integrity ecosystem, not as a standalone enforcement mechanism.

**AI Literacy:**

> Tian has become an advocate for AI literacy alongside detection — arguing that students and educators need to understand what AI is and how it works in order to use it responsibly. His public profile (NPR, The Daily Beast, Princeton Princetonian) has contributed to mass AI literacy.

### Khung pháp lý & Đạo đức

**Khung quốc tế:**

> GPTZero is used in 40+ countries and is the de facto standard AI detection tool in education globally. Tian has engaged with education ministries, accreditation bodies, and international academic integrity organizations. His tool is referenced in academic integrity policy discussions worldwide.

### Góc nhìn EdTech

**AI tutor vs AI cheater:**

> Tian is squarely on the 'AI as cheater' detection side — his entire product exists to identify AI-generated text that students submit as their own. However, Tian has expressed nuanced views: he does not oppose all AI use in education, but believes undisclosed AI submission undermines the learning contract.

**Chính sách EdTech companies:**

> GPTZero is itself an EdTech company. Tian has worked with Turnitin, major LMS providers, and school districts to integrate detection into existing EdTech infrastructure. His company's policy evolution — from simple detection to nuanced writing reports — reflects EdTech's challenge in balancing detection accuracy with false-positive harm.

**Đề xuất nhà nghiên cứu EdTech:**

> Tian recommends: (1) use AI detection as one layer in a multi-pronged integrity approach — not a standalone solution; (2) invest in false-positive mitigation, especially for ESL writers; (3) combine detection with assessment redesign and AI literacy education; (4) develop industry standards for AI detection accuracy and transparency; (5) advocate for AI companies to implement watermarking to make detection more reliable; (6) engage policymakers on AI detection ethics — due process before punishing based on detection output.

### Nguồn tài liệu

**Bài báo khoa học:**

> Tian has not published traditional peer-reviewed papers — his contribution is the GPTZero tool and associated technical documentation. GPTZero's methodology is described in blog posts and technical documentation at gptzero.me. His work is cited extensively in academic integrity literature including Perkins et al. (2023), Newton & Xiromeriti (2023), and others.

**Sách tham khảo:**

> GPTZero technical documentation (gptzero.me); news coverage: NPR (Jan 9, 2023), The Daily Beast, The Princetonian; investor announcements (seed and Series A). Tian's Princeton thesis on AI detection.

## GPTZero {#gptzero}

| Field | Value |
|---|---|
| Type | tool |
| Url | https://gptzero.me |

### Định nghĩa & Khái niệm

**Định nghĩa:**

> GPTZero is an AI-generated content detection tool created by Edward Tian, a Princeton University computer science and journalism student, launched January 2, 2023 — one of the first public AI detectors following ChatGPT's November 2022 release. It analyzes text using a proprietary 7-component model based on perplexity (unpredictability of word choices) and burstiness (variation in sentence-level complexity) to generate a probability score for AI authorship. GPTZero is positioned as an educator-focused tool and has been widely adopted in K-12 and higher education. As of 2024, it claims 2.5 million+ educator users globally.

**Phân loại vi phạm:**

> GPTZero detects AI-generated text as a post-submission analysis tool. It produces: (1) an overall document-level AI probability score; (2) sentence-level highlighting of likely AI-written sentences; (3) a 'completely AI' / 'mixed' / 'human' classification. It is designed to flag ChatGPT, GPT-4, Gemini, Claude, Llama, and other major LLMs. Like Turnitin AI Detection, it cannot prevent AI use — only detect it probabilistically after submission.

**Lịch sử khái niệm:**

> GPTZero was launched January 2, 2023 as a personal project by Edward Tian while a junior at Princeton. The tool went viral immediately (over 30,000 users in 48 hours after a tweet by Tian). It preceded Turnitin AI Detection (April 2023) and OpenAI's since-retracted classifier (January 2023, retired July 2023) as one of the earliest widely-used AI detectors. Tian subsequently co-founded GPTZero Inc. and received venture funding. The tool has been updated continuously since January 2023.

### Bằng chứng thực tế

**Tên trường hợp:**

> GPTZero — commercial AI detection tool. Creator: Edward Tian (Princeton University, CS + Journalism). Company: GPTZero Inc. (Princeton/New York). Users: 2.5M+ educators as of 2024. Key controversy: Liang et al. (Stanford HAI, 2023) found 61.3% false positive rate when testing GPTZero against TOEFL essays written by non-native English speakers — the majority flagged as AI-generated were actually human-written.

**Diễn biến:**

> GPTZero's core algorithm: perplexity measures how well a language model predicts each word given context — AI-generated text typically has lower perplexity (more predictable word choices); burstiness measures variation in sentence complexity — human writing has high variation (some complex, some simple sentences) while AI writing tends toward more uniform complexity. The 7-component proprietary model (as of 2024 description) adds additional features beyond perplexity and burstiness, including sentence-level analysis and domain-specific adjustments [specific components not publicly disclosed]. The tool has been updated to detect specific LLMs and evolved from binary classification to probabilistic scoring.

**Kết quả xử lý:**

> GPTZero has become widely used in K-12 and some HE contexts in the US, UK, and internationally. However, the Stanford HAI finding (61.3% false positive rate on TOEFL essays) created significant controversy. The false positive problem is particularly acute for: (1) ESL writers — who tend toward simpler, more uniform sentence structures that mimic AI patterns; (2) scientific and technical writing — which has low perplexity by necessity; (3) students who write in a formal academic register. GPTZero has acknowledged the ESL false positive problem and added 'ESL mode' adjustments [uncertain effectiveness of adjustment]. Multiple education advocacy organizations (including ETS, NAACP) have expressed concern about discriminatory impact.

**Con số thống kê:**

> Creator: Edward Tian (Princeton University). Launch date: January 2, 2023. Users: 2.5M+ educators as of 2024. Claimed false positive rate: 0.24% (GPTZero marketing claim). Empirically measured false positive rate on TOEFL essays: 61.3% (Liang et al. Stanford 2023, published PLOS ONE 2023). Method: 7-component proprietary model (perplexity + burstiness + additional features). Viral growth: 30,000+ users in first 48 hours (January 2023).

### Tác hại & Hậu quả

**Hại cho người học:**

> The 61.3% false positive rate on TOEFL essays is the most documented harm: ESL writers who produce legitimate human-written work are falsely flagged as AI cheaters at extremely high rates. For Vietnamese students writing in English (on international programs, in English-medium courses, or applying to international institutions), GPTZero poses a specific fairness risk. A Vietnamese student writing a carefully structured English essay may be falsely accused of AI fraud based on writing patterns that reflect non-native English proficiency rather than AI generation.

**Hại cho nghiên cứu:**

> GPTZero is positioned as an educator tool, not a research integrity tool, but its false positive pattern has implications for research: Vietnamese researchers writing in English for international journals may produce text that GPTZero (or Turnitin AI Detection using similar methods) would incorrectly flag. Journal peer reviewers using informal AI detection may reject legitimate submissions from non-native English researchers based on false positive AI detection.

**Hại cho xã hội:**

> The 61.3% ESL false positive rate creates systemic inequity: AI detection tools built on English-language training data penalize non-native English writers for linguistic characteristics that reflect language learning backgrounds, not dishonesty. This is a form of algorithmic discrimination with significant implications for educational equity in non-English-speaking countries. At scale, if widely deployed in Vietnamese HE, GPTZero-class tools would produce systematic false accusations against Vietnamese students.

### Phát hiện & Công cụ

**Công cụ phát hiện:**

> GPTZero is itself a detection tool. Technical method: perplexity scoring + burstiness analysis + 7-component proprietary model. Supported LLMs: GPT-3.5, GPT-4, Claude, Gemini, Llama family, and others. Accuracy on native English academic writing: moderate (better than claimed 0.24% FP but worse than suggested in marketing materials). Accuracy on ESL writing: very poor (61.3% FP by Liang et al.). Accuracy on heavily human-edited AI content: low — the arms race with humanization tools (Quillbot, Undetectable.ai) means that GPTZero can be evaded by students who apply post-generation editing.

**Hạn chế công cụ:**

> Key limitations: (1) 61.3% false positive rate on TOEFL essays (Liang et al. Stanford 2023) — catastrophically high for enforcement use; (2) Claimed 0.24% FP rate vs. empirical measurements 250x higher — significant discrepancy between marketing and reality; (3) Cannot detect human-edited AI (students who rewrite AI output extensively evade detection); (4) Subject to arms race — humanization tools like Undetectable.ai, HIX.AI specifically train against GPTZero's detection signals; (5) Short texts (under 300 words) have low reliability; (6) Domain specificity — technical, scientific, and formal academic writing has inherently lower perplexity, increasing false positive risk in these contexts; (7) Proprietary model — no independent verification of the 7-component system.

**LLM Watermarking:**

> GPTZero's statistical detection method would be superseded by LLM watermarking if major LLMs implemented production watermarking. A cryptographic watermark embedded by OpenAI, Google, or Anthropic at generation time would be more reliable than GPTZero's pattern analysis — though the need for closed-source LLM cooperation makes this a policy challenge as much as a technical one.

### Phòng ngừa & Giải pháp

**Thiết kế bài tập:**

> GPTZero's ESL false positive problem argues most strongly for assessment redesign over detection-based enforcement in Vietnamese HE contexts: if 61.3% of Vietnamese students' English-language TOEFL-equivalent essays would be falsely flagged, using GPTZero as a primary enforcement tool would create systematic injustice. Process-based assessment (Google Docs history, staged drafts, oral defense) avoids the ESL discrimination problem entirely.

**Văn hóa tổ chức:**

> GPTZero's viral growth and creator's narrative (Princeton student fighting AI cheating) made it culturally resonant in the media. This cultural positioning created adoption among educators who felt they needed a response to AI fraud but did not critically evaluate the tool's accuracy. McCabe's culture-first approach would counsel educators to build integrity culture rather than reaching for a technically flawed detection tool — but the emotional urgency of AI cheating concerns drove rapid adoption.

**AI Literacy:**

> GPTZero's website and educational materials frame the tool as part of an AI literacy ecosystem — helping educators understand AI capabilities and limitations. However, the tool's accuracy limitations mean that teaching 'how to detect AI writing' using GPTZero risks teaching incorrect AI literacy (overconfidence in detection). True AI literacy for educators includes understanding that current detection tools have significant limitations and cannot be used as sole evidence of AI fraud.

### Khung pháp lý & Đạo đức

**Khung quốc tế:**

> The Liang et al. Stanford finding (61.3% FP on TOEFL essays) has been widely cited in international discussions of AI detection equity. UNESCO 2023 guidance on GenAI in education explicitly flags AI detection tool bias as a concern. EU AI Act's non-discrimination provisions (Article 10, bias in AI training data) could theoretically apply to detection tools used in educational decisions — GPTZero's ESL bias would be a prima facie compliance concern if deployed in EU HE contexts.

**Pháp luật Việt Nam:**

> No Vietnamese legal framework addresses AI detection tools. Vietnamese institutions using GPTZero operate without legal guidance on acceptable accuracy standards, student rights in false positive cases, or required disclosure of detection methods. Given Vietnamese students' higher false positive risk (ESL writing patterns), deployment without policy guidance creates significant unfairness and institutional liability.

### Góc nhìn EdTech

**AI tutor vs AI cheater:**

> GPTZero cannot distinguish between AI-as-tutor and AI-as-cheater any more than Turnitin can. A student who used GPT for drafting assistance and substantially revised the output (legitimate AI tutoring use) may evade detection; a Vietnamese student who wrote entirely in their own hand but produces lower-perplexity English may be falsely flagged (false AI cheater accusation). The tool operationalizes a technically inaccurate binary.

**Chính sách EdTech companies:**

> GPTZero Inc. has commercial relationships with some school districts and districts in the US. The business model (free tier + premium institutional tier) creates tension between maximizing adoption and ensuring accuracy — aggressive marketing of the 0.24% false positive claim vs. empirical 61.3% on TOEFL essays represents a potentially misleading commercial practice.

**Đề xuất nhà nghiên cứu EdTech:**

> As an EdTech researcher: (1) Strongly advise against GPTZero as primary enforcement evidence in Vietnamese HE — the ESL false positive rate makes it structurally discriminatory in Vietnamese contexts; (2) If instructors use GPTZero informally, require that any positive result triggers a conversation with the student, not automatic grade penalty; (3) Conduct local validation testing: test GPTZero against a corpus of Vietnamese students' authentic English writing to measure false positive rate in Vietnamese HE context specifically; (4) Include the Liang et al. finding in faculty AI literacy training as a cautionary case study; (5) Advocate for a Vietnamese government or MOET-level requirement that AI detection tools used in academic enforcement undergo bias audits against Vietnamese student writing samples before deployment.

### Nguồn tài liệu

**Bài báo khoa học:**

> Liang, W. et al. (2023) 'GPT detectors are biased against non-native English writers.' Stanford HAI / PLOS ONE, doi:10.1371/journal.pone.0285423. Weber-Wulff, D. et al. (2023) testing of AI detection tools. Perkins, M. et al. (2023) 'Academic integrity considerations of AI LLMs.' Journal of Academic Ethics. Tian, E. (2023) original GPTZero technical blog post (January 2023).

**Sách tham khảo:**

> Eaton, S.E. (2024) Second Handbook of Academic Integrity. Cotton, D. et al. (2023) 'Chatting and cheating: Ensuring academic integrity in the era of ChatGPT.' Innovations in Education and Teaching International.

### Other Info

**Company:** GPTZero Inc.

## Turnitin AI Detection {#turnitin-ai-detection}

| Field | Value |
|---|---|
| Type | tool |
| Url | https://www.turnitin.com/products/features/ai-writing-detection |

### Định nghĩa & Khái niệm

**Định nghĩa:**

> Turnitin AI Detection is an AI-generated content detection feature added to Turnitin's existing plagiarism detection platform in April 2023. It analyzes submitted text to produce a percentage score indicating how much of the content may be AI-generated. The detection engine uses a proprietary model trained to distinguish human writing from large language model outputs, with a stated focus on ChatGPT and GPT-4 family outputs. Turnitin is the dominant plagiarism detection tool in higher education globally, used by 15,000+ institutions in 140+ countries.

**Phân loại vi phạm:**

> Turnitin AI Detection is a post-submission detection tool — it analyzes completed work rather than monitoring the writing process. It produces a probabilistic score (0-100% AI-written) at the document level, with sentence-level highlighting. It does not prevent AI use, only flags potential AI-generated content for instructor review. False positives (human writing incorrectly flagged as AI) are documented as a significant limitation, particularly for non-native English writers.

**Lịch sử khái niệm:**

> Turnitin launched its plagiarism detection service in 1997. AI detection was added April 2023, responding to the ChatGPT surge (Nov 2022). The feature was controversial from launch: accuracy claims (98%) were immediately challenged by independent testing (Washington Post found ~50% accuracy in testing, 2023). By late 2023, multiple major US universities (Vanderbilt, Michigan State, Northwestern, UT Austin) had disabled the AI detection feature while retaining plagiarism detection.

### Bằng chứng thực tế

**Diễn biến:**

> The detection feature works by analyzing text patterns, sentence structures, and statistical distributions characteristic of LLM outputs. Turnitin claims a training dataset of human and AI writing across subjects. The tool generates a percentage score and flags individual sentences it identifies as AI-generated. Instructor workflow: review flagged submissions, use percentage as input to a holistic judgment (Turnitin explicitly states the score is not definitive proof of AI use). The tool is integrated into the existing Turnitin submission flow — instructors access AI scores alongside similarity/plagiarism scores.

**Kết quả xử lý:**

> Multiple institutions reported student complaints about false positives (human work incorrectly flagged). Vanderbilt, Michigan State, Northwestern, UT Austin disabled the AI detection feature while retaining Turnitin's plagiarism detection in 2023. Some institutions (University of Sydney, UK Russell Group universities) continue using the feature with explicit instructor guidance to treat scores as probabilistic, not definitive. Turnitin has updated its model multiple times since April 2023 to reduce false positives [version history uncertain].

**Con số thống kê:**

> Official accuracy claim: 98% (Turnitin, April 2023). Washington Post independent test accuracy: ~50% (2023). False positive rate: ~4% sentence-level (Turnitin internal data, 2023). ESL writer false positive rate: ~30% higher than native English writers (Liang et al. Stanford 2023, applied to Turnitin-class tools). Independent audit false flag rate: ~7% (specific audit source uncertain). Universities disabled: 4 major US universities publicly disclosed in 2023 (Vanderbilt, Michigan State, Northwestern, UT Austin). Global user base: 15,000+ institutions, 140+ countries (legacy Turnitin platform).

### Tác hại & Hậu quả

**Hại cho người học:**

> False positives harm innocent students disproportionately: students who write in non-standard registers (ESL writers, students with dyslexia using assistive tools, students working in languages other than their native tongue) are flagged at higher rates. The Liang et al. finding of 30% higher false positive rates for ESL writers has direct implications for Vietnamese students writing in English on international programs. A false positive can result in academic integrity investigation, grade penalty, or reputational damage — all for work the student legitimately produced.

**Hại cho nghiên cứu:**

> Turnitin's application to research submissions (via iThenticate, Turnitin's research-focused product) raises similar concerns: non-native English academic writers face higher false positive risk. Additionally, the tool's inability to detect AI content that has been substantially human-edited means that sophisticated AI use in research goes undetected while legitimate human writing may be flagged.

**Hại cho xã hội:**

> University abandonment of the tool (Vanderbilt, Michigan State, etc.) signals institutional distrust of commercial AI detection claims — a trust deficit that affects the entire AI detection market. The public controversy has created a narrative that no detection tool is reliable, potentially normalizing the view that AI fraud is undetectable and therefore unenforceable.

### Phát hiện & Công cụ

**Công cụ phát hiện:**

> Turnitin AI Detection uses a transformer-based classifier trained to distinguish human vs LLM-generated text. The model analyzes perplexity (how predictable each word is given context), burstiness (variation in sentence complexity), and stylistic patterns. It updates periodically as new LLMs emerge. The tool is most accurate for pure, unedited AI output; accuracy degrades significantly for human-edited AI content or AI-assisted (not AI-generated) writing.

**Hạn chế công cụ:**

> Key limitations: (1) False positive rate ~4% at sentence level — in a 1,000-sentence document, ~40 sentences may be incorrectly flagged; (2) ESL writer false positive rate 30% higher (Liang et al. 2023) — structural disadvantage for non-native English writers; (3) 7% false flag rate in independent audit [source uncertain]; (4) Cannot reliably detect human-edited AI content (arms race: humanization tools like Quillbot, Undetectable.ai, HIX.AI can rewrite AI output to evade detection); (5) WaPo testing found ~50% accuracy — far below Turnitin's 98% claim; (6) Model trained primarily on English — performance on non-English submissions uncertain; (7) Score is probabilistic, not definitive — requires instructor judgment for enforcement, introducing inconsistency.

**LLM Watermarking:**

> Turnitin's detection is based on post-hoc statistical analysis, not on LLM watermarking. If LLM watermarking (Kirchenbauer/Aaronson schemes) were deployed by OpenAI and Google, Turnitin could integrate watermark verification as a more reliable detection method than its current statistical approach. As of 2025, no major LLM has deployed production watermarking, so Turnitin continues to rely on statistical pattern analysis.

### Phòng ngừa & Giải pháp

**Thiết kế bài tập:**

> Turnitin's limitations support the case for assessment redesign: if detection is ~50% accurate in real-world conditions and ESL students are disproportionately harmed by false positives, the appropriate response is not better detection but assessment designs where AI substitution is not possible (oral defense, real-time production, verified portfolio). Using Turnitin AI detection as the primary enforcement mechanism creates an unacceptable false positive risk for equitable outcomes.

**Văn hóa tổ chức:**

> The controversial deployment trajectory reflects a broader cultural tension: detection-first institutions (enforce via technology) vs. values-first institutions (build culture, use technology only as support). The universities that disabled Turnitin AI detection are disproportionately research-intensive institutions with strong honor code traditions (Northwestern, Vanderbilt) — consistent with McCabe's finding that honor code cultures rely less on external enforcement tools.

**AI Literacy:**

> Turnitin's deployment without adequate AI literacy education for students creates unfair enforcement: students receive penalty-level consequences for a behavior they may not fully understand is prohibited. AI literacy programs should precede or accompany AI detection deployment — students need to understand what AI detection tools do, their limitations, and why AI fraud is harmful before detection enforcement is applied.

### Khung pháp lý & Đạo đức

**Khung quốc tế:**

> UNESCO 2023 GenAI guidance and EU AI Act Article 50 (transparency obligations) are relevant: if AI detection tools are used in enforcement decisions affecting students, the tools themselves may need to meet accuracy and bias standards analogous to those in the EU AI Act's high-risk AI system provisions (Annex III). Turnitin's documented ESL bias would fail such a bias audit. The IEEE EAD principles require that AI systems used in high-stakes contexts demonstrate documented, audited performance.

**Pháp luật Việt Nam:**

> Decree 109/2022 does not address detection tools. Vietnamese institutions using Turnitin AI detection operate without legal guidance on acceptable false positive rates, required accuracy standards, or student rights in false positive scenarios. This creates legal exposure for Vietnamese universities that apply grade penalties based on AI detection scores without explicit policy authority.

**Chính sách châu Á:**

> NUS and NTU (Singapore) use Turnitin but have separate AI integrity frameworks that do not rely solely on Turnitin's AI detection. CUHK's 2023 expulsions [uncertain if Turnitin was used in those cases] — Asian HE institutions generally use detection as one input among multiple factors rather than as definitive evidence.

### Góc nhìn EdTech

**AI tutor vs AI cheater:**

> Turnitin AI Detection cannot distinguish between AI-as-tutor (student used AI for learning support, drafting assistance, feedback) and AI-as-cheater (student submitted AI output as their own). A student who used ChatGPT extensively as a learning tool and then wrote their own essay may receive a false positive; a student who used AI to generate content but then heavily edited it may evade detection entirely. The tool operationalizes a binary (AI/human) that does not reflect the spectrum of actual AI-assisted learning.

**Chính sách EdTech companies:**

> Turnitin is itself an EdTech company whose business model depends on institutional subscription revenue from plagiarism and AI detection. The commercial incentive to claim high accuracy (98%) conflicts with the academic integrity community's need for honest capability assessment. The contrast with Coursera's post-submission dialogue approach (which requires no detection tool) illustrates the difference between enforcement-based and culture-based EdTech approaches to academic integrity.

**Đề xuất nhà nghiên cứu EdTech:**

> As an EdTech researcher: (1) Do not recommend Turnitin AI Detection as a primary enforcement mechanism given the documented ESL false positive rate — particularly relevant for Vietnamese student contexts; (2) If using detection tools, implement tiered review: automated flag → instructor review → conversation with student → institutional decision; never apply grade penalty based on score alone; (3) Advocate for independent bias audits of all AI detection tools before institutional deployment; (4) Use the WaPo 50% accuracy finding to argue for assessment redesign over detection escalation; (5) If Vietnamese institutions adopt AI detection, recommend starting with Turnitin AI Detection scores as advisory (no grade consequence) while building evidence for local accuracy under Vietnamese HE conditions.

### Nguồn tài liệu

**Bài báo khoa học:**

> Liang, W. et al. (2023) 'GPT detectors are biased against non-native English writers.' Stanford HAI, PLOS ONE 2023. Washington Post (2023) independent evaluation of AI detection tools. Perkins, M. et al. (2023) 'Academic integrity considerations of AI large language models.' Journal of Academic Ethics. Weber-Wulff, D. et al. (2023) 'Testing of detection tools for AI-generated text' — comparison study.

**Sách tham khảo:**

> Eaton, S.E. (2024) Second Handbook of Academic Integrity — chapter on technology-mediated detection. Cotton, D.R.E. et al. (2023) 'Chatting and cheating: Ensuring academic integrity in the era of ChatGPT.'

**Nguồn Việt Nam:**

> Low reported adoption in Vietnamese HEIs [uncertain institutional count]. HCMUFA case (2023) — detection method was instructor judgment, not Turnitin AI. Vietnamese universities with Turnitin contracts likely have access to AI detection feature but deployment guidance status unclear.
